A Tor-ON run hit **161s**: a single STUCK Tor exit circuit wedged a per-range enhancement connect ~58s (`enhance_s=62` vs 0.53 direct; `Failed to obtain exit circuit … tor operation timed out`), freezing the UI at ~61% (the ChainTip→Historic range boundary). Diagnosed as **Tor circuit weather, NOT the lift** — `enhance/scheduler/connector/grpc/scan` are byte-identical since pre-lift (verified by `git diff 8d4a51fe HEAD`); the first lucky Tor run (90s) just drew fast circuits. **FIX** (`connector.rs` `TorConn::connect`): bounded per-attempt circuit-build timeout (`TOR_CONNECT_TIMEOUT=15s`) + retry on a **fresh isolated circuit** (`TOR_CONNECT_ATTEMPTS=3`) — a stuck circuit is cut at 15s and the next attempt draws a new circuit, instead of blocking on arti's long internal timeout; healthy case connects on attempt 1 with ~0 overhead. Failure stays `Transport` (transient) so every higher layer (scan treestate retry, per-range enhancement non-fatal defer, initial-pass never-Error loop) still stacks. Direct path + byte-identity untouched (the oracle never exercises Tor). ENGINE_BUILD → `2026-06-17.torretry`. Previous (T8.3.5): **T8.3.5 — warm fresh-start reporting DONE 2026-06-16 (SDK side; NEW UX regression the user reported during T8.x — cold launch flashed balance 0→X and the smart widget ~0%→hidden).** Pure-Swift, NO Rust/FFI change. Root cause: `SlipstreamSynchronizer` emitted a COLD initial state — `prepare()`→`[:]`/`.disconnected`, `start()`→`.syncing(0,false)`, and the first tick used PASS-LOCAL `counterProgress` (0% of the few new blocks on a small catch-up) — until `cachedSummary` + the live tip resolved seconds later. FIX (3 parts, all via new pure helpers in `+PureHelpers.swift`): (1) `prepare()` seeds `cachedSummary` via `getWalletSummary` + emits the WARM `initialState` (real balance + truthful progress from the summary; cold `.disconnected` only when there's no summary = a genuinely fresh wallet); (2) `start()` seeds its initial `.syncing` from `cachedSummary` (not 0%); (3) the `.syncing` tick FLOORS the pass-local counter with the wallet's GLOBAL summary progress — `syncingProgress = max(counterProgress, summaryProgress)` — so a catch-up reads ~100% (widget stays hidden) while a real restore (summary ≈ 0 at the start) still climbs 0→100% off the pass-local counter. GATES: **`swift test --filter OfflineTests` 483/0** (477+6 new helper tests); SwiftLint zero NEW violations (kept `SlipstreamSynchronizer.swift` <600 lines by extracting `initialState`; fixed mixed-arg asserts). The **98% smart-widget visibility threshold + any last-balance display cache remain ZODL-SIDE** — the SDK now emits truthful balance + progress, so Zodl's existing >98%-hide logic does the rest (the user can confirm the 98% lives in Zodl). [needs-user] device confirm: rebuild Zodl (Swift-only — reuse the existing `t82-mempool` ios slice, NO FFI rebuild needed) + cold-launch a synced wallet → balance shows X immediately + no 0% widget flash. **NEXT → T8.4** A10 spam-era device-memory scaling.

New module `slipstream/core/src/mempool.rs` + `grpc::open_mempool_stream`. ENGINE_BUILD → `2026-06-15.t82-mempool`. **PRODUCTION BUG found + fixed during the darkside run:** lightwalletd sends `GetMempoolStream` response headers LAZILY (only with the first tx or the stream-close on a new block), so the open is now bounded by `MEMPOOL_SESSION_IDLE` (60s → benign `IdleReconnect`), NOT the 30s gRPC unary timeout — otherwise a quiet mempool (common; blocks ~75s apart) spuriously errors and, after the cap, wrongly disables mempool. `run_session` gained an `idle: Duration` param (FFI passes the 60s const; tests pass a short value). **GATES GREEN:** core 142+1ign / cli 27 / clippy ZERO both feature sets / darkside follow(2)+sync(5)+**oracle(3 byte-identical CLEAN)**=10 / FFI compiles default+gpu / mempool unit tests(2)+compile-time `SEEN_TXIDS_CAP` invariant + **real-server quiet-mempool integration test** (`IdleReconnect`, 0 received, 0 hits, no error — validates the lazy-header fix end-to-end). **DARKSIDE LIMITATION (Blockers):** darkside v0.4.9 `GetMempoolStream` does NOT serve `StageTransactions`-staged txs (empirically: 30s of active mempool polling = 0 delivery; the old-SDK Swift pending/mempool tests are themselves disabled, #1247/#1518) → the full wallet-HIT lifecycle is NOT hermetically testable here (the plan's Step-4 escape hatch). Covered instead by: the integration test above + the unit tests + the structural argument (run_session stores via upstream's audited `decrypt_and_store_transaction`, the OLD SDK's exact production write path, lib.rs:2070) + [needs-user] manual validation (CLI `--follow` mainnet + a real 0-conf send). **NON-PERTURBATION** proven via the hermetic byte-identical darkside oracle (3/3 CLEAN with T8.2 present) + the structural fact that mempool/follow code never executes during an oracle sync (oracle = engine keyless sync, no FFI handle / follow loop / mempool session) → the mainnet tip−2000 oracle is redundant for this additive surface and was NOT re-run (would only add network/tip-skew risk; documented, not skipped-by-guess). **DEFERRED (device-deploy, not correctness): OfflineTests + full 3-slice rebuild + [needs-user] field validation** — no FFI *surface* change (internal follow-loop only; `cargo check` green) and `init-local-ffi --macos-only` would clobber the iPad iOS slices needed for device testing; do these together when validating on device. **NEXT → T8.3** startFailed(.rustSlipstreamNotOpen) launch wart (2 SDK-side guards in SlipstreamSynchronizer.start()/stop(), zero Zodl changes; fact G).

Previous (strategy fork — user is sleeping on it, 2026-06-15): **BOTH PERF SPIKES PARKED + LEARNINGS LOGGED 2026-06-15. Strategy fork open (user). See `docs/slipstream/2026-06-15-perf-spikes-learnings.md`.** GPU (Phase B) AND persist-pipelining both built, measured, FALSIFIED on modern devices (same root cause: compute-bound, `wall ≈ total_cpu_work/cores`; concurrency levers conserve work + add core contention → GPU −25% / depth-4 −26% on M4). The magnitude (12×) was the ENGINE, already shipped. Both KEPT, parked: GPU = `gpu` feature default-off (zero wgpu shipped, fully isolated); pipelining = `persist_depth` default 1 = byte-identical (live refactor, NOT feature-gated — can revert/gate for stricter isolation). **The ONLY remaining lever is WORK REDUCTION (algorithmic): fewer combines (build/retain less commitment tree) or faster decrypt (SIMD/batch) — both reduce `total_cpu_work` so they CAN lower the floor; harder + riskier + correctness-critical. DECISION (user): pursue a work-reduction spike, or stop at 12×-on-the-compute-floor (an excellent place).** Below = the parked spikes' detail (GPU matrix, pipelining R1–R3).

Parked detail follows:

**GPU PARK STATUS:** B0 COMPLETE (byte-identical GPU Orchard combine, mainnet VERDICT IDENTICAL, KAT 0/10k, oracles green) + v0.3 OFFLOAD toggle exists (`gpu` cargo feature default-OFF → released libzcashlc has zero wgpu; `gpu_subtree`/`ZCASH_GPU_SUBTREE` default-false; `rebuild-local-ffi.sh --gpu`). All banked, byte-identical-when-on, reusable. **Dropped/not-built: B1.2 cooperative split, ③ Sapling kernel, B1.4 scan-hook.** WHY PARK: v0.3 device matrix proved GPU-subtree is a NARROW ~1.1× win (A14/iPhone) that REGRESSES both extremes (A10 pathological; M4 −25% — combine parity 6.2s but GPU adds scan-contention), gating needs net-benefit (hard), and the combine just isn't a big enough slice of a scan/persist-bound sync. A14 v0.3 run now OPTIONAL (doesn't change park). The whole GPU detour's payoff = the measurement below.

**NEXT SPIKE — persist-pipelining (in-our-control ~22%, NOT external/SQLite/GPU/theoretical):** stage-split decomposition (all 3 modern devices) shows **persist_busy > scan-compute** everywhere → persist (≈60% combine, the bit GPU couldn't speed) is the TRUE bottleneck, only partially hidden by the **depth-1** write-behind. Current total sits ~22% above the `max(scan,persist)` perfect-overlap floor on EVERY device: iPhone 89.7→floor 70s; A14 125.5→98s; M4 32.3→25s. Lever: **deepen the write-behind pipeline (depth-2/3+, per-device memory budget)** so scan stops stalling on dense combine chunks → walk toward the floor → ~10–22%, byte-identical (timing not content; golden-oracle gated). **FLOOR IS NOT A HARD LIMIT:** the max(scan,persist) floor assumes CURRENT work; sub-floor (e.g. iPhone <60s) needs REDUCING work — combine algorithm (build/retain less of the tree) or scan decrypt (SIMD/batch/parked-L4a) — a stack ON TOP of pipelining. **SPEC+PLAN WRITTEN + APPROVED (autonomous, full-autonomy mandate): `plans/2026-06-15-persist-pipelining-design.md`.** Design: lane → bounded pending-queue (depth N) + serial persist worker; persist stays in-order ⇒ byte-identical by construction; nullifier view advances per-chunk ⇒ spend-detection depth-independent; per-device depth budget (N=1 A10 / 3–4 iPhone-M4-A14). **R1 DONE + VERIFIED 2026-06-15.** `PersistLane` refactored to a bounded `VecDeque<(span, PersistJob)>` + `spawn_next`/`pending_count` (serial, in-order; `depth` field, default 1). `submit_job` = push → spawn-if-idle → block only when `pending_count > depth`; `drain` loops the queue; commit error CLEARS the queue (range aborts, queued dropped). At depth=1 it's byte-for-byte the old behaviour. GATES ALL GREEN: 5 lane contract tests (incl. submit-awaits-previous, error-precedence, panic-poison) + no-deadlock stress + core 140+1ign/cli 26 + clippy both sets + **full darkside serial suite (14, incl. the byte-identical write-behind oracle) CLEAN**. **R3 RESULT: THESIS FALSIFIED — deeper write-behind REGRESSES the M4 (~26% SLOWER). Persist-pipelining PARKED. The ~22% "floor headroom" was an analysis artifact.** M4 CLI A/B (273k, same binary): depth-1 = 33.4s vs depth-4 = **42.2s (1.26× slower)**. persist_wait dropped (22.0→19.1, scan DID run ahead) but **scan-active DOUBLED (10.8→22.6s)** = core contention. Root cause: on ≥6-core devices the persist commit uses the GLOBAL rayon pool (all cores); scan also uses all cores; deeper buffering runs them CONCURRENTLY → they fight for the same cores. depth-1's backpressure was beneficially serializing them. The `max(scan,persist)` floor assumed scan+persist on SEPARATE resources — false; the cores are shared, so the real floor is `total_cpu_work / cores`, which pipelining can't reduce (only reshuffles + adds contention). **IDENTICAL root cause to the GPU M4 regression: modern devices are compute-bound; concurrency levers (GPU offload, deeper pipeline) don't help — they conserve work + add contention.** R1+R2 KEPT but PARKED: `persist_depth` defaults to 1 = today's behaviour exactly (byte-identical, verified green), depth>1 has no proven benefit (regresses on high-core; might help only isolated-lane-pool low-core A10 = constrained/gate-off). Oracle hit tip-skew (mainnet moved during the 4-sync run) — byte-identicalness already covered by R1's darkside oracle + the structural serial/in-order argument; moot since depth>1 isn't a win. **STRATEGIC: both concurrency levers exhausted on modern devices. The ONLY remaining lever is WORK REDUCTION (algorithmic): fewer combines (build/retain less of the commitment tree) or faster decrypt (SIMD/batch). Harder + riskier; a new spike + a user strategy check (two negative results = inflection).**

**R2 DONE + VERIFIED 2026-06-15.** `EngineConfig.persist_depth` (default 1, validate 1..=64) → `PersistLane::open(.., depth)` (production `scan_chunks` uses `config.persist_depth`; treestate/oracle/test paths pass 1). CLI `sync --persist-depth` + `oracle --persist-depth-b`. Gates: core 140+1ign / cli 27 (new `persist_depth_flags_parse`) / clippy both sets. **NOW EXECUTING R3** (M4 CLI A/B: `sync --persist-depth 1` vs `4` for the time delta + `oracle --sparse-b --write-behind-b --persist-depth-b 4` for byte-identical VERDICT IDENTICAL) → R4 (FFI + device). → R2 (`EngineConfig.persist_depth` + CLI flag, allow N>1) → R3 (M4 CLI A/B depth-1 vs depth-4: byte-identical + measure %) → R4 (FFI + device A/B). Then work-reduction spikes (combine algorithm / decrypt) for sub-floor. Previous: B0.2 **DONE** 2026-06-15 — `gpu_subtree.rs` (feature `gpu`): `precompute_shard_map` (complete shard bottom-up, batched `orchard_combine_batch` per level, keyed `(level‖l‖r)→parent`) + `GpuHashOrchard` newtype (`Hashable` reading a thread-local map, CPU fallback on miss) + `build_subtrees_gpu` (reuses `from_iter` VERBATIM, then a recursive `convert_tree` rebuild). **B0.2 finding (Decision Log):** shardtree's `LocatedTree::map` maps only the leaf value `V`, NOT the annotation `A`, and `Tree`'s inner `Node` is `pub(crate)` — so the `GpuHashOrchard→MerkleHashOrchard` convert is a recursive rebuild via the public `Tree::{parent,leaf,empty}` + `Deref<Node>` (not a one-line map). Correctness rests on the CPU fallback (any combine `precompute` misses is CPU-computed → byte-identical regardless of from_iter padding). GATE `gpu_subtree_build_matches_cpu`: GPU shard == CPU `from_iter` for n∈{1,7,1000,1024,2000,5000} (incl. partial/padded). clippy clean both feature sets; default core 140+1ign / cli 24. `build_subtrees`/`BUILD_CHUNK_SIZE` → pub(crate).

Previous: **T6.8-H2 DONE 2026-06-12 — transient-error retries SHIPPED (ENGINE_BUILD=2026-06-13.retry1): scan-path treestate retry gap CLOSED; pass-level bounded retry shipped. Field failure: mid-scan chunk 3361166 scanned fine, then `zcashlc: slipstream sync failed err=transport: get_tree_state: timed out after 30s` → whole sync dead at 6.1%. Fix A: retry_get_tree_state (grpc.rs) — up to 3 attempts (TREESTATE_RETRY_MAX=2), backoff 1s/3s, RECONNECT on each retry; applied to initial treestate seed in scan_chunks (scan.rs:169) AND per-chunk-boundary prefetch spawn (scan.rs:222–229). Non-transient errors (decode/proto/logic) propagate immediately. Fix B: bounded pass-level retry in FFI runner (rust/src/lib.rs) — wrap sync_once in a loop (PASS_RETRY_MAX=2, total 3 attempts), sleep 5s then 15s (pass_retry_sleep), only Transport/is_transient errors retry, SyncState stays Syncing during retries (% briefly resets over remaining work — acceptable UX, noted), SyncStarted emitted once, should_retry pure function unit-tested. is_transient on SlipstreamError: Transport=true; Config/Wallet/ScanContinuity/Discontinuity/MisbehavingServer/Stopped=false (exhaustive 7-test coverage). Progress %-restart note: begin_pass() at each sync_once entry resets ratio counters (scanned/fetched/pass_total/range_end/spendable) — correct because re-suggest returns remaining ranges; monotonic delta counters (enhanced_txs/ranges_completed/reorgs_recovered) stay untouched across retries. NEXT: user iPhone re-test (recent wallet sanity + 935000 old-wallet sandblasting acceptance, iPhone only) → then T6.9 L4b write-behind pipelining.** See session log 2026-06-12 T6.8-H2 entry.

Previous: **T6.8-S DONE 2026-06-12 — byte-budgeted adaptive chunk splitting SHIPPED (ENGINE_BUILD=2026-06-13.sandblast-split): the sandblasting era (mainnet ~1.70M–2.00M) is now TRAVERSABLE. Field gap (user, birthday-935000 wallet, Mac-class): 10k chunks carry 346,271 / 1,237,313 / 2,536,689 outputs ≈ hundreds of MB → `chunk fetch timed out; retrying` looped forever at index 80-84 (≈ heights 1.735–1.785M) since a giant chunk can never finish in the 120s whole-chunk timeout and each retry restarted it from scratch; 300–500 MB single-chunk RAM → device jetsam. FIX (density-adaptive, NO hardcoded heights): fetch workers split each streamed plan chunk into ≤`chunk_split_bytes` (default 8 MiB, CLI `--chunk-split-bytes`) sub-chunks; reorder key extended to (plan_index, sub_index)+is_last; the 120s deadline now bounds ONE sub-chunk's accumulation (reset on every emission — a healthy-but-huge chunk can never time out); retries RESUME from the last emitted height (already-released heights never re-sent → double-scan structurally impossible); new AheadGate (8×split ahead-byte budget, floor-exempt) bounds reorder-buffer memory. Mac acceptance (600s timed observation, fresh TEST_UFVK wallet birthday=1,700,000, zec.rocks, defaults): 84,964 sandblasted blocks / 671 sub-chunks / 43.2M outputs traversed at ≈8.9k blk/min THROUGH the exact field-failure region — plan chunks split into up to 131 subs / 1,032 MB per 10k blocks; 6 mid-stream server EOF cuts ALL resume-recovered; 0 timeout loops, 0 failures. RSS grew 1.31→1.84 GB across the window (256 MB-wire queue budget × decode amplification + allocator HWM + per-range in-memory sparse tree) — fine on Mac, but DEVICE memory budgets need spam-era tuning before A10-class devices cross 1.7M (recorded in Blockers). NEXT: user iPhone re-test with the hardened+sandblast build (verify tag `2026-06-13.sandblast-split` in the device log); then T6.9 L4b write-behind pipelining.** See session log 2026-06-12 T6.8-S entry.

Previous: **HARDENING1 DONE 2026-06-12 — field-failure diagnosis + failure-path hardening SHIPPED (ENGINE_BUILD=2026-06-13.hardening1). Failure 1 `rustSlipstreamSyncFailed(3375119)` payload DECODED: the associated value is the snapshot CHAIN TIP at failure time (SlipstreamSynchronizer.swift:365 passes `snap.chainTip`), NOT an error code — it proves the pass got past preflight (tip fetched + DB updated) and failed later; the REAL error is only in device os_log (`tracing::error!` at the FFI runner; the tag=4 event value is a hardcoded generic 1). Mac repro attempt: 10/10 CLEAN tip-area fresh restores (5×zec.rocks, 5×eu.zec.rocks, incl. streams=8/chunk=1k) — cluster desync NOT reproduced, stays plausible-unconfirmed as failure-1 trigger. Failure 2 (frozen at 3.7% = exactly 1 chunk, state Syncing forever): rayon::join EXONERATED by hermetic stress (120 joins under 1/2-thread local pools + 2-thread global cold-injection, CLEAN); root cause is (1) un-timed transport await on a stalled-but-open connection (treestate prefetch scan.rs awaits had NO deadline) or (2) silently swallowed task panic (JoinHandle was never inspected) — BOTH now structurally impossible: B1 panic supervisor (Error(2) + tag=4/value=2 event + tracing hook), B2 30s deadlines on every unary/stream await, B3 reorg backoff (500ms×n cap 3s), B4 Swift 120s stall watchdog (loud Logger.error, no auto-restart). NEXT: user iPhone re-test with hardened build; then T6.9 L4b write-behind pipelining.** See session log 2026-06-12 hardening1 entry.

| T6.9b dedicated lane thread pool — write-behind contention fix (#1755) | **done** | T6.9 field evidence (iPad A10 log 2026-06-13): with write-behind ON the lane's `rayon::join` (+ upstream `build_subtrees`' `par_chunks`) ran on the GLOBAL rayon pool — the same pool saturated by concurrent decrypt. On 4-core A10: lane busy inflated ~60% (417.8s vs ~258s inline; smoking gun: tree wall 12.9s vs per-pool sum 6.7s on one chunk = tasks queueing behind decrypt); depth-1 persist_wait inflated to 259.3s; net write-behind gain only ~20s vs ~120s projected. Fix (persist.rs, PersistLane): `LANE_POOL_THREADS=2` dedicated `rayon::ThreadPool` created at `PersistLane::open()` (one pool per lane lifetime); `submit_job`'s `spawn_blocking` closure runs via `lane_pool.install(|| …)`. Rayon scoping guarantees ALL nested rayon usage inside the closure — the `rayon::join` (sapling∥orchard, T6.8-L3a) AND upstream `build_subtrees`' `par_chunks` — lands on the lane's 2-thread pool, zero interaction with the global decrypt pool. Why 2 threads: preserves intra-commit sapling∥orchard parallelism (T6.8-L3a gain) while giving decrypt full access to the other 2 A10 cores; a 3rd thread adds no parallelism (only 2 independent closures exist in the join) and fights decrypt. Inline path (write_behind=false) UNTOUCHED — it calls `sparse_put_blocks` directly, never concurrently with decrypt, so no contention issue. Honest-physics note: total CPU is conserved on a saturated chip — this removes scheduling pathology (queueing/interleaving inflation), not adds cores; 5:00-5:30 iPad projection is labeled PROJECTION. Mac before/after (50k, tip=3375640, zec.rocks, 2×before + 4×after): BEFORE scan_s 5.61/5.55 (mean 5.58s), persist_wait 3.13/3.09 (mean 3.11s), persist_overlap 1.10/1.14 (mean 1.12s); AFTER scan_s 11.02/12.35/10.82/10.72 (mean 11.23s, larger because Mac's global pool had 10+ threads for commit while lane pool limits to 2), persist_wait 8.18/8.31/8.36/8.24 (mean 8.27s), persist_overlap 1.18/1.12/1.10/1.12 (mean 1.13s) — overlap unchanged (consistent with physics: Mac commit > decrypt so overlap was already capped by commit wall time); Mac regression is expected and documented. Tests: +1 unit (`lane_pool_has_configured_thread_count` — asserts `current_num_threads()==LANE_POOL_THREADS`); stress_sparse_join CLEAN (stress test was already written for the global-pool layering; with lane pool the rayon install in spawn_blocking changes the pool for all nested work — no deadlock). Core 139+1ign; cli 23; stress 1/1. Clippy ZERO both feature sets. Hermetic oracles CLEAN (write-behind variants). Darkside 14/14 serial. Mainnet tip−2000 oracle --sparse-b --write-behind-b VERDICT IDENTICAL (birthday=3373641, tip=3375640). ENGINE_BUILD → "2026-06-13.l4b-lanepool". init-local-ffi.sh full 3 slices + tag probe; OfflineTests 475/0 SPM-purged. |
| T6.9 L4b write-behind persistence pipelining (#1755) | **done — flag-gated, default OFF** | Depth-1 overlap of chunk N's commit with chunk N+1's decryption behind `EngineConfig::write_behind` (CLI `--write-behind`; validate: requires sparse). READ-SURFACE RECON (the task's heart — enumerated, every site cited): scan_cached_blocks (zcash_client_backend-0.23.0 data_api/chain.rs:586-664) touches the DB in exactly FOUR reads + ONE write: (1) get_unified_full_viewing_keys chain.rs:603-605 → per-range cache (put_blocks never mutates accounts); (2) block_metadata(from_height−1) chain.rs:614-620, consumed by check_hash_continuity (scanning/compact.rs:191-221, prev-hash reorg detection) + PositionTracker::for_compact_block (compact.rs:400-516, prior tree sizes) → served from pending tail via ScannedBlock::to_block_metadata() — the IDENTICAL value upstream threads intra-call at chain.rs:653; (3)+(4) get_{sapling,orchard}_nullifiers(Unspent) via Nullifiers::unspent scanning.rs:360-368 ← chain.rs:623 → running unspent view seeded from DB at range start, advanced per stashed block with upstream's own update_with semantics (scanning.rs:435-464; SQLite Unspent semantics verified at zcash_client_sqlite-0.21.0 wallet/common.rs:155-189: nf NOT NULL ∧ tx mined ∧ not spent-by-mined) — a note FOUND in pending chunk N is visible to chunk N+1's spend detection; re-seeded after every enhancement barrier (enhancement stores full txs). Every OTHER WalletRead/WalletWrite method on the WriteBehindFacade fails LOUDLY ("not part of the scan_cached_blocks read surface") — read-surface drift can never become a silent stale read. TREE OWNERSHIP: SparseTreeState moved INTO the PersistLane (second WalletDb connection to the same WAL file); insert+flush stay together inside each deferred commit = the EXACT sparse_put_blocks transaction, strictly serial (ownership ping-pongs through spawn_blocking — structural, not advisory); scan side never touches tree or DB mid-range; no concurrent writers by construction (scan reads virtualized; enhancement/truncate/suggest only behind drain barriers). Barriers: range end + ALL error exits + interleaved-enhancement points = drain in-flight + propagate (persist error precedence over ScanContinuity, documented); submit(N+1) awaits N first (depth-1 backpressure; failure aborts the range before another unit is submitted). Instrumentation: per-chunk persist_wait_ms (chunk log + per-await "write-behind persist awaited" line with busy+span); stage-split gains persist_wait_s + persist_overlap_s (=busy−wait clamped); scan_s stays honest loop wall (documented in engine.rs comment); rows/tree/flush attribution unchanged inside the deferred commit. TESTS+GATES: +24 hermetic (17 persist: nullifier-delta incl. found-in-pending-N-spent-in-N+1, facade tail/known-absent/loud-errors/depth-1-stash-guard, lane serial/backpressure/error-before-next-submit/drain/panic-poison; 2 WB oracles; 2 config; 4 CLI — taking core 118→138+1ign, cli 19→23); hermetic WB oracles CLEAN (3×1000 synthetic + dense ChunkSplitter boundaries); darkside 14/14 serial (10 existing flag-OFF no-regression + 4 NEW: write_behind_pipeline_matches_upstream_on_darkside_fixture [REAL-notes semantic_diff CLEAN], sync_finds_fixture_transactions_write_behind, fixture_funds_are_spendable_after_full_sync_write_behind, reorg_recovery_produces_correct_tip_write_behind [probe THROUGH the WB facade → ScanContinuity{at:663201} → truncate 663191 → tip 663202]); mainnet tip−2000 oracle --sparse-b --write-behind-b VERDICT IDENTICAL (birthday=3373563, tip=3375563, 26 tables OK); clippy ZERO both feature sets; Mac 50k A/B ×3 pairs: OFF 6.66/6.91/6.81 (mean 6.79s) vs ON 7.35/5.66/5.54 (mean 6.18s, pair-1 polluted by 2× fetch_s server variance) — persist_overlap_s ≈1.01/1.05/1.06 consistent, per-chunk decrypt drops ~1s→~250-380ms with commit deferred, persist_wait ~3.0s/run (Mac commit ≈790ms > decrypt ≈320ms → Mac is persist-bound; the lever's real target is the device's ~1:1 balance); ENGINE_BUILD → "2026-06-13.l4b-writebehind"; init-local-ffi.sh full 3 slices + tag probe; OfflineTests 475/0 SPM-purged. | T6.3b checkpoint-downgrade spike (controller NO-GO follow-up) | **done — REVISED GATE: GO** | Hypothesis confirmed: T6.3's tree_ms was upstream's checkpoint-churn algorithm (one `Retention::Checkpoint` per scanned block, ~10k/chunk, pruned back to 100 on every insert_tree), not insert hashing. Each closure returns Result; both checked after join (no unwrap/expect). Immutable per-pool inputs (sap_start/orch_start, sap_frontier/orch_frontier, frontier_checkpoint_id) captured before the split-borrow. tree_ms reports JOINED wall time. Mac bench (tip−50k, birthday=3324600, 6 chunks): tree_ms 340–534ms/chunk (T6.6 baseline: 526–648ms); scan_s=6.73s vs T6.6's 7.49s; total 7.19s. Mac gain modest (~10% scan_s): both pools are already CPU-balanced on an M-series Mac; device gain expected larger (2 under-utilized cores on A10/A15). Oracle: VERDICT IDENTICAL (tip−2000, --sparse-b, tip=3375044). Hermetic: 82+1ign core + 17 cli; clippy 0 both feature sets. Darkside 10/10 serial. OfflineTests 470/0 (SPM purge). init-local-ffi.sh full (3 slices); layout canonical; freshness: ios-arm64=1, ios-sim=2, macos=2. L2 seam: see Decision Log 2026-06-12. |

| T5.1 instrumentation | done | Chunk.outputs (u64) added to chunk.rs — counts sapling outputs + orchard actions via one pass in from_blocks; per-chunk timing (elapsed_ms) added to scan.rs in both scan_chunks and scan_chunks_from_treestate (info! logging); stage-split (total_s, fetch_s, scan_s, enhance_s, blocks, bound) added to engine.rs sync_once tail (info! logging). Test: chunk_outputs_counts_vtx_outputs_and_actions (2 synthetic blocks with known vtx counts, assert sum). Suites: cargo test -p slipstream-core -p slipstream-cli 47/0; clippy clean both feature sets; darkside feature compiles. CORRECTION (P5 review): chunk-level INFO elapsed was initially only in scan_chunks_from_treestate; production scan_chunks carried per-sub-batch debug timing only — fixed in the P5 closing commit (chunk_elapsed_ms accumulator in both info! lines). |
| T5.2 adaptive scan sub-batching | done | Controller: fn next_batch_len(prev_len, prev_elapsed_ms, target_ms, min, max) → proportional adjust prev_len*target/elapsed, clamped [min,max]; TARGET_BATCH_MS=3_000, MIN_BATCH=1_000. Production scan_chunks splits each chunk into sub-batches; batch_len initialized from first chunk's full length (fast-path unchanged: on fast hardware batch_len grows to/stays at chunk size → exactly one scan call per chunk). Treestate threading: per sub-batch, prefetch spawned for sub_end before blocking scan; awaited after; becomes from_state for next sub-batch — upstream assert from_state.height+1==from_height satisfied at every boundary (proof in code comment). scan_chunks_from_treestate left whole-chunk (comment explains: synthesized states can't split mid-chunk). Per-sub-batch debug! log (batch_start, batch_end, batch_len, elapsed_ms); chunk-level info! log retained at chunk granularity. RPC cost: ~1 GetTreeState per ~3s on slow devices, zero extra on fast devices. 6 controller unit tests: clamps_to_max, grows_on_fast_device, shrinks_on_slow_device, clamps_to_min, zero_elapsed_returns_max, stable_when_on_target. Suites: cargo test -p slipstream-core -p slipstream-cli 53+14/0; clippy clean both feature sets; darkside --no-run compiles. P5 review: from_state threading verified correct at all boundaries; detached-prefetch comment corrected. |
| T5.3 summary throttle | done | While state==Syncing (1), wallet-summary refetch interval becomes SUMMARY_SYNC_INTERVAL=8s (state ticks stay 2s from cheap counters; cached summary bridges between fetches). All other states keep 2s. Implementation: extracted static helper summaryFetchInterval(forState:UInt8) → TimeInterval (pure function, no side effects); updated kickSummaryFetchIfNeeded(state:) to accept and use the current snapshot state. Done (state 3) path unchanged: immediate .synced without waiting. Tests: 5 new unit tests (testSummaryIntervalWhileSyncingIs8s + 4 other-state branches) verify interval selection. Suites: swift build clean; swift test --filter OfflineTests 450/0 (includes 5 new tests); all pass. |

## Phase P4 — iOS Docking (plan: `plans/2026-06-11-phase-4-ios-docking.md`)

| Task | Status | Session notes |
|---|---|---|
| T4.9 regression fixes (non-blocking poll + switchTo no-op) | done | User regression: NEW wallet (36k blocks, 14 txs) showed long pauses (0%→24%→55%→done) after T4.8. TWO root causes investigated and fixed. H-A CONFIRMED: getWalletSummary() is @DBActor-isolated (global serial executor) and runs multiple heavy SQL queries: per-note balance walk with v_sapling_shard_unscanned_ranges JOIN, transparent balance, shard root iteration — all serialised on DBActor. In tickPoll() it was awaited directly, blocking every tick: if the call takes 10–30 s mid-scan, no state update fires until it returns → UI shows "0% for a long time → 24% → long pause → 55%". Evidence: zcash_client_sqlite-0.20.2/src/wallet.rs:2450–2820 (get_wallet_summary fn). H-B PARTIAL: AutoServerSelectionLiveKey.swift line 33 already guards `guard best.host != current.host || best.port != current.port else { return }` before calling switchIfIdle — so no unconditional restart. However, if best != current early on, switchTo fires mid-scan and restarts the pass (adds latency cost). F2 adds safety at the SDK layer. Fix F1 (non-blocking poll): decoupled tickPoll from getWalletSummary. tickPoll now emits state EVERY tick from cheap snapshot + cachedSummary (last completed fetch). A separate background Task (kickSummaryFetchIfNeeded) fetches the summary at most once every 2 s with a 3-second hard timeout (withTaskTimeout helper, nanoseconds-based for iOS 13+/macOS 12+ compat). Only ONE fetch in flight at a time (summaryTask guard). On state==3 (Done): cancel in-flight task + emit .synced IMMEDIATELY without waiting for summary. stopPolling() also cancels the summary task. wipe() resets cachedSummary+lastSummaryFinishDate. Fix F2 (switchTo no-op on same endpoint): compare host+port+secure before any engine ops; if identical → log debug + return. Fix F3 (switchTo mid-sync warning): log warn "switchTo during active sync — pass will restart" when wasRunning==true. Helper withTaskTimeout<T>(nanoseconds:UInt64, operation:) is `internal` (not private) so @testable test targets can exercise it directly. New tests (4): testWithTaskTimeoutReturnsValueWhenFasterThanDeadline, testWithTaskTimeoutThrowsWhenDeadlineExceeded, testSwitchToSameEndpointIsNoOp, testSwitchToDifferentEndpointIsNotNoOp. Suites: swift build clean; OfflineTests 445/0 (+4 vs T4.8 441); cargo 77/0. Zodl: no changes needed (F2 covers the same-endpoint case at SDK layer; H-B showed existing guard in AutoServerSelectionLiveKey.swift already prevents unconditional restart). User should observe: continuous progress emissions every 2 s during sync; no long pauses; immediate .synced on completion. Commits: SDK [#1755] slipstream: T4.9 regression fixes. |

## Decision log (append-only)

- 2026-06-10 — D1–D9 locked in ROADMAP.md (in-repo workspace; upstream-brain M1; unchanged data.db; additive Swift seam; CLI-first; keyless engine; engine-owned tokio runtime; poll-based FFI; branch/commit policy). - 2026-06-10 — Zodl iOS repo confirmed: `/Users/lukaskorba/Dev/Xcode/GitHub/LukasKorba/secant-ios-wallet` (remote `git@github.com:LukasKorba/zodl-ios.git`), read/write + git permission granted by user. Observed on feature branch `MOB-1125-implement-multi-currency-conversion-support`, clean tree — P4's `slipstream` branch must be cut from Zodl's main.
- 2026-06-10 — T2.3-spike (ChainState threading) resolved: ChainState comes from server treestate at each chunk boundary, prefetched concurrently with the previous chunk's scan (zero added latency); local derivation rejected — zcash_client_sqlite exposes no frontier read API.
- 2026-06-10 — T2.7 baseline spike outcome (record): Tests/PerformanceTests/SynchronizerTests.swift is not headlessly runnable (disabled test prefix + unreachable default endpoint); old-SDK baseline therefore deferred to P4 A/B per plan contingency. G2 marked with slipstream absolutes only.
- 2026-06-10 — Stage-split timing (fetch vs scan) was NOT instrumentable in T2.7: SyncReport carries counts, not per-stage wall-clock. Input for T3.0: the P3 event/snapshot surface must add per-stage timing so G5 device runs can report bound honestly.
- 2026-06-11 — D7 deviation (T4.1): tokio Runtime is created at `zcashlc_slipstream_open` and lives until `zcashlc_slipstream_free` drops the Box<SlipstreamHandle>. `stop()` cancels only the sync task; the runtime remains alive for the next `start()` call. This mirrors the TorRuntime precedent (lib.rs:3157-3195) where the runtime is owned by the handle for its full lifetime. Alternative (create per-start) was rejected: it would leak tasks in flight if stop() raced with start(), and would make snapshot()/drain_events() unavailable between start/stop cycles.
- 2026-06-11 — cbindgen dep-crate resolution (T4.1): cbindgen in build.rs does NOT have parse_deps=true; it only parses the root lib.rs. The plan's C4/C12 correction correctly identified this — but the fix implemented was to define FfiSlipstreamSnapshot + FfiSlipstreamEvent DIRECTLY in rust/src/lib.rs (additive), rather than enabling parse_deps (which would parse all workspace deps and risk spurious header entries). SlipstreamHandle's event ring stores slipstream_core::ffi_handle::FfiSlipstreamEvent; the drain function converts to the lib.rs-defined type by field-copy (identical repr(C) layout). This is a minor implementation deviation from the plan's preferred approach but achieves the same result.
- 2026-06-11 — tokio added to root Cargo.toml [dependencies] (T4.1): libzcashlc uses arti/PreferredRuntime for Tor but tokio was not a direct dep of the libzcashlc package before this task. The zcashlc_slipstream_open function needs tokio::runtime::Builder; adding `tokio = { workspace = true }` to root [dependencies] resolved the compile error.
- 2026-06-11 — D1 containment extension (T4.4): Tests/OfflineTests/Slipstream* and Tests/DarksideTests/Slipstream* are added as enumerated exceptions to the D1 containment rule (test files are additive). SlipstreamFFI.swift received a memberwise SlipstreamSnapshot init (additive, no deletion) to avoid test targets importing libzcashlc directly. Error files touched additively only: ZRUST0097 (rustSlipstreamUnsupported) added to ZcashErrorCodeDefinition.swift, ZcashError.swift (case+message+code arm), ZcashErrorCode.swift; zero deletions confirmed (git diff | grep '^-' = empty).
- 2026-06-11 — T6.3 NO-GO verdict: sparse-tree persistence facade implemented and measured. A/B ratio = 0.96 (sparse SLOWER). Root cause: shardtree computation cost (~6-7s/chunk for insert_tree + prune_excess_checkpoints at ~35k outputs/chunk) is the same regardless of persistence backend — it runs in both paths. The SQLite shard I/O that sparse was meant to eliminate is fast in absolute terms (~300ms/chunk rows_ms in the baseline); the tree CPU work dominates. The preload-all-shards seed step adds ~200-500ms overhead at chunk 1. flush_ms=1ms confirms the flush bet was correct in isolation — but isolation was the wrong analysis: the bottleneck is shardtree's own O(log N * K) work, not the persistence calls surrounding it. Consequence: the sparse-tree strategy (intercept put_blocks, accumulate in memory, flush once per chunk) does not address the real bottleneck. The decrypt-kernel/column-ECDH track (ROADMAP P6 original T6.3 column) is the appropriate next investigation: the bottleneck is shardtree CPU during insert_tree, and the correct lever is reducing per-output tree operations (via commitment-scheme changes, batch insertion, or parallel subtree construction). shardtree/incrementalmerkletree/zip32 promoted to direct slipstream-core deps as part of T6.3 implementation — these remain in place as they are now transitive deps used in persist.rs.
- 2026-06-11 — T5.6 design choices: (1) F1 whole-pass denominator: set_pass_total(scanned_so_far + sum(all_returned_ranges)) stores the complete denominator atomically on each re-suggest; deprecated add_pass_total (accumulate per-range caused 0→100→60% snap-back). (2) F2 boundary summary: 20s timeout (vs 3s idle) for kickBoundarySummaryFetchIfNeeded; separate boundarySummaryTask (one-in-flight guard); exempt from no-summary-while-syncing rule; cachedSummary feeds balances into subsequent Syncing ticks. (3) F3 interleaved enhancement: per-range run_enhancement in scheduler (fresh gRPC client per call, same pattern as engine.rs final run); per-range EnhanceStats accumulated in SyncReport.enhance (sum); engine's final post-loop run also merges into same field; enhance_s in stage-split = total across all runs. K-chunk hoisting NOT done (restructuring not clean in <1h, per-range is sufficient requirement). Darkside suite: unchanged paths (scheduler enhancement path exercises new code). `add_pass_total` kept as deprecated shim (store semantics).
- 2026-06-11 — T5.6 review hardening (controller): (1) Per-range interleaved enhancement is NON-FATAL by design — it is an optimization (progressive tx visibility), not a correctness guarantee; the final post-loop enhancement is the guarantee. Rationale: the FFI runner is single-shot (sync_once error → SyncState::Error → sync halts until app-lifecycle retry); a transient gRPC blip at a range boundary must not abort a 16-minute restore. (2) Per-pass counter semantics: Progress counters split into RATIO counters (scanned, fetched, pass_total, range_end, spendable — reset by `begin_pass()` at sync_once entry) vs DELTA counters (enhanced_txs, ranges_completed, reorgs_recovered — monotonic per HANDLE because Swift detects changes via strict-greater last-seen comparisons; resetting them would break foundTransactions emission and boundary-summary triggers on resumed passes). The handle outlives passes (prepare() opens once; stop()/start() reuse), so this split is load-bearing for interruption-resume correctness.
- 2026-06-11 — T6.3b checkpoint-downgrade design (REVISED GATE: GO). The T6.3 NO-GO attribution (tree_ms ≈ 6500ms/chunk in BOTH paths) was upstream's checkpoint-churn ALGORITHM, not insert hashing: scanning emits `Retention::Checkpoint{id}` for EVERY scanned block (scanning.rs:777-786), and every `insert_tree` call re-runs `prune_excess_checkpoints` (max 100 = PRUNING_DEPTH at ll/wallet.rs:52), so ~9,900 checkpoints per 10k-chunk are created then destroyed, each cycle paying O(count) `with_checkpoints` walks + `clear_flags` shard walks. Fix (persist.rs, inside the existing `sparse_persistence` flag — no new flag): downgrade doomed checkpoint retentions BEFORE they enter the tree. Identity argument (verified at source level, then empirically): (a) prune residue — removing a checkpoint clears only the CHECKPOINT flag at its leaf (shardtree lib.rs:550-660); MARKED survives, all else becomes EPHEMERAL → pre-mapping Checkpoint{Marked}→Marked / Checkpoint{other}→Ephemeral leaves identical leaf flags (Marking::Reference never occurs in scan streams; the from_state frontier insert keeps its Reference checkpoint untouched). (b) retained set — upstream's iterative oldest-first prune over an ascending id stream retains exactly the newest-100 DISTINCT ids of (stored ∪ frontier ∪ new); the per-pool cutoff is that set's minimum (`doomed_checkpoint_cutoff`), NOT the controller-sketched naive `last_scanned − 100`: a pool with <100 checkpoints in the height window (sparse sapling stretches on modern mainnet) retains OLDER ids, and a fixed-height cutoff would diverge from upstream's final checkpoint tables. (c) ensure_checkpoints must consume FULL pre-downgrade position maps (`stream_checkpoint_positions`, equivalent to upstream's post-build extraction per shardtree batch.rs:204): a filtered `existing` map would remap an ensure-height whose nearest own-pool checkpoint lies below the cutoff onto the frontier position; the upstream-verbatim `height > min_cp` add-filter then yields the identical surviving add-set because post-insert min_cp equals the cutoff-set minimum in both paths. (d) physical shard shape converges: CHECKPOINT flags do not block unite-merging (only MARKED/REFERENCE do, prunable.rs:358-372) and clear_flags re-unites cleared paths bottom-up. Measured (Mac, 50k): tree_ms 6500→498-600ms/chunk, scan_s 34.63→6.85 (5.05×), flush_ms still 1ms, downgraded ~6.2-6.8k/chunk. Oracles: hermetic 3×1000 CLEAN; real-range tip−2000 IDENTICAL with downgraded=1104. Scope guard: `sparse_persistence` default remains FALSE; T6.4 must keep the downgrade and prove it at scale (50k mainnet oracle + darkside fixtures + reorg/truncate interplay with the per-range SparseTreeState).

- 2026-06-12 — T6.8-L3b NO-GO verdict (orchard-tree targeted fix): sub-attribution falsified every prescribed design before code was written. The expected dominant bucket (insert_ms with per-insert checkpoint bookkeeping) measures 15% with ZERO checkpoint-churn residue (downgrade_ms=ensure_ms=0 — T6.3b already removed it); checkpoint-free inserts would risk shard-blob identity for ≤26s device upside. build_ms (~48%) is the Merkle tree itself: shardtree batch.rs from_iter/unite prune-hashes every ephemeral sibling pair (≈1 hash/leaf, values persisted in blobs) — count-irreducible, already parallel. frontier_ms (~33%) is a FIXED per-put_blocks cost (proved by the limit=1 tail chunk: 146-204ms with zero build/insert) caused by shardtree-0.6.2 merge_checked (prunable.rs:277-352) recomputing root_hash of BOTH merge sides at EVERY recursion level with no annotation write-back and discarding the result (.ok() + vacuous zip().all()) whenever either side is incomplete — which is ALWAYS for frontier path-trees. Fix requires patching/forking shardtree (same seam class as the L2 raw-SQL block) — recorded as a potential L4 lever, NOT taken here. Skipping per-chunk insert_frontier was analyzed and rejected: the call's effects (re-opening pruned paths along the frontier, ommer annotations, REFERENCE retention flags, frontier checkpoint row) are present in upstream's final blobs and must be replicated. Chunk-size increase rejected on A10 memory grounds. Sub-attribution instrumentation kept permanently (per-pool split at info level) — every future device log now carries the device's own sub-split. Remaining device levers (P6-next): decrypt-kernel track (L4), scan∥tree pipelining (needs a crash-consistency design — tree flush must stay transactional with rows), shardtree fork.

- 2026-06-22 — [#1755] **importAccount re-scan progress visibility FIXED** (Slipstream Swift, additive; NO engine/Rust change — engine already re-scans correctly via `suggest_scan_ranges`). **Bug:** on an already-synced wallet, `importAccount` (esp. old birthday) adds a large `[birthday, tip]` recovery range, but syncing-time % is `max(passLocalCounter, summaryFloor)` where `summaryFloor` = the CACHED `WalletSummary`, deliberately never refreshed mid-pass (T5.5 zero-summary-while-syncing perf rule) → the floor stays ~1.0, MASKS the whole re-scan, and the SmartBanner (shows only < 0.98) never appears → sync invisible. Compounding: the follow loop only re-syncs on tip advance (`session.rs` `should_resync`), so the re-scan also waited ≤ ~75 s for the next block. **Fix** (`SlipstreamSynchronizer.importAccount`): after import, (1) AWAITED cached-summary refresh (20 s boundary timeout; DB quiet on an idle wallet) so the floor reflects the new recovery range (`recoverUntil=chainTip` → it lands in `recoveryProgress`, which `composeProgress` sums into the denominator) and drops < 0.98; (2) `start()` restart so the engine re-scans NOW and emits `.syncing(lowFloor)` immediately — refresh BEFORE restart so the first emission already carries the low value (no 100%→low flicker). **Tests:** 3 pure (`SlipstreamOfflineTests` — `testSyncingProgressLargeRescanMaskedByStaleFloor` / `…VisibleWithFreshFloor` / `testComposeProgressFreshlyImportedOldBirthdayIsBelowBannerThreshold`); full OfflineTests 539/0; engine cargo green; + darkside `SlipstreamDarksideTests.testImportAccountRescanPathRoundTrip` (real FFI import → refresh → restart on a running synchronizer; survives + keeps emitting). **Limitation:** lightwalletd v0.4.9 (no AddTreeState) can't full-sync → the progress-DIP end-to-end is NOT auto-testable on darkside; the user's manual mainnet repro is the acceptance gate. Swift-only ⇒ ENGINE_BUILD unchanged.
- 2026-06-22 (cont.) — [#1755] **importAccount fix REVISED v1 → v2** after field "no change". v1 refreshed the summary then floored on it; FIELD FAIL because `getWalletSummary` right after `importAccount` still reports ~100% — the scan queue is updated for the new account only by the next pass's `update_chain_tip`, NOT by `import_account_ufvk` — so the floor stayed high and masked the re-scan. **v2 instead CLEARS the cached summary** (`cachedSummary = nil` → floor 0) so the pass-local counter drives a real 0→100% climb regardless of summary timing; balances fall back to `latestState` and the pass's boundary/Done fetches repopulate. Added a `[#1755] importAccount … cleared progress floor; isRunning=…` `logger.debug` so the next field run is conclusive (proves the path ran + the SDK rebuild picked up the change + the `isRunning` branch). Routing verified: wallet `SDKSynchronizerLive.importAccount` → `synchronizer.importAccount` → `SlipstreamSynchronizer` (when `useSlipstreamSynchronizer` flag on). Tests re-green (539 OfflineTests + darkside round-trip). Still Swift-only; manual mainnet remains the acceptance gate.
- 2026-06-22 (cont.) — [#1755] **importAccount fix v2 → v3** (field-log diagnosis). The user's mainnet log PROVED v2's path runs (`[#1755] importAccount` debug line fired ⇒ build current) and the engine re-scans the new account (`transparent refreshing UTXOs account_id=<new> start_height=2700001`; restart pass + `chain tip updated` + suggested ranges). But STILL no SmartBanner — because after the `start()` restart the engine spends a moment in **Tor-bootstrap = state Disconnected**, and `tickPoll`'s idle branch (`kickSummaryFetchIfNeeded`) re-fetched `getWalletSummary` (still ~100% pre-`update_chain_tip`) and **re-raised the floor BEFORE scanning**, overwriting v2's one-time `cachedSummary = nil`. **v3:** a `forceCounterProgressUntilDone` flag set by importAccount; `tickPoll` state==1 uses `counterProgress` directly (no floor) while set; cleared on state==3 (Done) so later small catch-ups still floor (no T8.3.5 regression). Default false ⇒ all existing behavior unchanged. Tests re-green (67 Slipstream + 539 OfflineTests + darkside round-trip). Swift-only; manual mainnet remains the gate.

- **(P8 GAP 1, user-reported 2026-06-12 — RESOLVED 2026-06-16, T8.2) Mempool detection** was missing entirely: sending from a non-Slipstream wallet to a Slipstream wallet showed NO incoming tx until it mined AND a new sync pass ran. SHIPPED: `mempool::run_session` (open `GetMempoolStream` → dedupe → upstream `decrypt_and_store_transaction(mined_height=None)` → relevance-gated → `progress.add_enhanced` → Swift `foundTransactions`) wired into the T8.1 follow loop as the between-pass mempool-or-sleep head; non-fatal. Two notes carried forward as limitations below: (a) the darkside-mempool test limitation, (b) the [needs-user] field validation.
- **(T8.2 DARKSIDE LIMITATION, recorded 2026-06-16) darkside v0.4.9 `GetMempoolStream` does NOT serve `StageTransactions`-staged txs as mempool entries.** Empirically confirmed: with a tx staged-but-unapplied at tip+1 (and applying a block at tip+1 to close), 30s of active darkside mempool polling (`"Refreshing mempool"` every ~2s in the server log) delivered NOTHING and the stream never closed within 30s. Also discovered: lightwalletd sends `GetMempoolStream` response headers LAZILY (only with the first tx / stream-close), so wrapping the OPEN in the 30s gRPC unary timeout spuriously fails a quiet mempool — FIXED (the open is bounded by `MEMPOOL_SESSION_IDLE`=60s → benign `IdleReconnect`, not error). Consequence: the full mempool→stored-hit→converge-on-mine lifecycle is NOT hermetically testable on darkside v0.4.9 (the old-SDK Swift pending/mempool tests are themselves disabled, #1247/#1518; the send-path needs a spending key the engine tests don't have). The T8.2 darkside test (`mempool_session_handles_quiet_mempool`) instead proves the real-server plumbing + the lazy-header open fix (quiet mempool → `IdleReconnect`, 0 received, 0 hits, no error). The wallet-HIT path rests on: the mempool.rs unit tests + the structural argument (run_session stores via upstream's audited `decrypt_and_store_transaction`, the OLD SDK's exact production write path) + the [needs-user] field validation below. Would be retired by a darkside ≥v0.5 / zaino with working mempool, or a CLI `--follow` send-test harness.
- **(T8.2 — ✅ VALIDATED ON DEVICE 2026-06-16) Field validation of mempool 0-conf:** PASSED on the user's device against the real mainnet mempool. Log evidence: `engine_build="2026-06-15.t82-mempool"` (freshness confirmed — not stale), 3× `mempool transaction not wallet-relevant` (relevance gate filters live mempool traffic correctly), and the key line **`mempool transaction stored (0-conf wallet hit) txid=a13e90a17fa5dd4fc795576d06d07f53c99eba6714f8427adf27680ffe86ff57`** = an incoming wallet tx surfaced 0-conf while still unmined. `gpu_subtree=false sparse=true write_behind=true` = production path. GAP 1 closed end-to-end. Also corroborates T8.1 (the `engine pass starting` line + between-pass mempool sessions prove the follow loop is live while foregrounded at tip) — T8.1's explicit mined-settlement confirmation rides the same session.
- **(P8 GAP 2, user-reported 2026-06-12, IMPORTANT) No periodic tip-following**: the FFI runner is single-shot per `start()` (one `sync_once`, then state=Done until app lifecycle restarts it). A wallet sitting open in foreground sees no new blocks. Old SDK re-synced continuously. Fix shape: engine-level re-arm loop (sleep ~20-30s at tip, re-run sync_once — cheap passes: suggest returns tiny/no ranges) or Swift-side restart-on-Done timer; begin_pass() (T5.6 review) already makes repeated passes counter-safe.
- (send-flow observation, user 2026-06-12, deprioritized BY USER) Sending from Slipstream WORKS end-to-end (received on the other side) but the sending screen felt slower than old SDK. User: "we don't need to address feeling it takes more time" — sending is an independent component. No action; revisit only if it resurfaces in P8 QA.
- **(FIELD FAILURE 1, user iPhone 16 Pro 2026-06-12, DIAGNOSED 2026-06-12 — hardened, awaiting re-test)** `[ZRUST0096] rustSlipstreamSyncFailed(3375119)` at ~60% of a 270k fresh restore (L3b-treesplit build). Payload semantics decoded: 3375119 is the snapshot CHAIN TIP at failure time (tickPoll passes `snap.chainTip` into the error; the FFI tag=4 event value is a hardcoded generic 1) — it localizes the failure to AFTER preflight (connect/subtree-roots/update_chain_tip all succeeded) but does NOT identify the failing stage; the real error text lands only in device os_log (`slipstream sync failed` error line). Mac repro: 10/10 clean tip-area restores against both zec.rocks and eu.zec.rocks — cluster tip-desync (ScanContinuity cap burn) stays the lead hypothesis, unconfirmed. Hardened: B3 reorg backoff gives desynced clusters a heal window; next failure will carry better evidence (B1 hook logs panics; ask the user for the os_log error line on repro). **T8.7 UPDATE 2026-06-16:** the resilience fix addresses this failure CLASS end-to-end — a transient sync failure (preflight OR mid-scan, initial OR follow) no longer surfaces a hard `.error`/`rustSlipstreamSyncFailed`; the wallet retries (showing `.disconnected` pre-sync / staying `.synced` in follow) with capped backoff. Only NON-transient errors + panics surface `.error`. So testers stop seeing this regardless of the underlying transient cause (the os_log `err=…` still pinpoints the RPC for root-causing).
- **(FIELD FAILURE 2, user iPhone 16 Pro 2026-06-12, DIAGNOSED 2026-06-12 — hardened, awaiting re-test)** UI frozen at 3.7% (= exactly one 10k chunk of ~270k) with state stuck Syncing, no logs, no error. Root-cause class proven structural: the spawned FFI sync task's JoinHandle was NEVER inspected (a panic died silently leaving state Syncing forever) AND multiple transport awaits had NO deadline (initial treestate scan.rs + per-sub-batch prefetch await, all unary calls, subtree-root/taddress/utxo streams — a stalled-but-open connection hung them forever). rayon::join EXONERATED (A5 stress: 120 joins under 1/2-thread constrained pools incl. production cold-injection layering — clean). Both mechanisms now impossible: B1 supervisor surfaces panics as Error-state + event; B2 deadlines turn stalls into retryable/fatal Transport errors; B4 watchdog makes any residual silent stall loudly visible in logs. **(T6.8-H2 UPDATE 2026-06-12)**: treestate retry gap NOW CLOSED — scan-side treestate calls now retry (3 attempts, reconnect) so a 30s stall shrugs off instead of failing the pass; pass-level bounded retry (PASS_RETRY_MAX=2) further softens the "no periodic tip-following" gap's failure mode (a transient stall at ANY point in a pass retries the whole pass up to 3 times). Note: pass-level retry does NOT implement periodic tip-following (GAP 2 still open); it only provides retry resilience for transient stalls.
- **(SANDBLASTING FIELD GAP, user 2026-06-12, RESOLVED 2026-06-12 — T6.8-S)** Old wallets (birthday < ~1.7M) could NOT traverse the spam era: 10k-block chunks at mainnet 1,700,001+ carry 346,271 / 1,237,313 / 2,536,689 outputs (≈ 0.2–1 GB wire per chunk) → `chunk fetch timed out; retrying worker_id=N index=80-84 attempt=1/2/3` looping forever (whole-chunk 120s timeout unmeetable; every retry restarted the giant chunk) + 300–500 MB single-chunk RAM (device jetsam). RESOLVED by byte-budgeted sub-chunk splitting + deadline-reset + resume-from-height retry (T6.8-S row, session log). Mac acceptance traversed the exact failing heights at ≈8.9k blk/min with zero timeout loops. REMAINING DEVICE FOLLOW-UP (P8/device-tuning): Mac RSS grew 1.31→1.84 GB through the dense era with default budgets (memory_budget_bytes=256 MB WIRE decodes to ~2-3×; allocator HWM; per-range in-memory sparse tree across a 1.5M-block range) — iPhone 16 Pro (8 GB) should survive, A10-class (2 GB) will NOT; before old-wallet restores on small devices, size memory_budget_bytes (e.g. 64 MB) + chunk_split_bytes (e.g. 4 MiB) per device class, and consider range-segmentation so the sparse tree state resets periodically. **✅ T8.4 SHIPPED 2026-06-16** the device-memory derate: Swift passes `ProcessInfo.physicalMemory` to `zcashlc_slipstream_open`, and `EngineConfig::scaled_for_device_memory` auto-applies the 64 MiB budget + 4 MiB split on <3 GiB devices (AheadGate auto-scales 8×split). [needs-user] A10 935000-birthday spam-era restore (the run that jetsams today) → survival = the acceptance gate; range-segmentation remains a future option if 64 MiB still isn't enough on the A10.
- **(L4b SHIPPED FLAG-OFF 2026-06-12, T6.9 — needs-user field validation)** Write-behind pipelining is oracle-clean and Mac-validated but ships default-OFF; the default-on decision waits on a device field A/B (`persist_wait_s`/`persist_overlap_s` in the device stage-split line are the evidence to read). Device A/B = one-line `cfg.write_behind = true;` at rust/src/lib.rs EngineConfig::new site + full rebuild + tag-verify, OR default flip after more soak. Mac analysis says the win lives on devices (decrypt≈persist balance), not on the Mac (persist-bound).
- **(L4a PARKED, user-requested 2026-06-12)** L4a decrypt-kernel (column-ECDH) track PARKED for future analysis — revisit after L4b write-behind pipelining.
- (T5.5 wart — ✅ RESOLVED 2026-06-16, T8.3) Zodl emitted `synchronizerStartFailed(.rustSlipstreamNotOpen)` at app launch before `prepare()`. ROOT CAUSE (fact G): `SlipstreamSynchronizer.start()` lacked the unprepared guard (vs `SDKSynchronizer.start`, SDKSynchronizer.swift:189-192) AND `stop()` emitted `.stopped` unconditionally — so Zodl's didEnterBackground unconditional `stop()` (RootInitialization.swift:75-76) during a background-hop-while-preparing forged `isPrepared` (`.unprepared`→`.stopped`), and the subsequent foreground `start()` then passed into `engine.start()` on a not-yet-open handle → `.rustSlipstreamNotOpen`. FIX: 2 SDK-side guards (start guards `isPrepared`→throws `.synchronizerNotPrepared`; stop only emits `.stopped` when `isPrepared`), zero Zodl changes. `.rustSlipstreamNotOpen` is now unreachable via the public Synchronizer API. OfflineTests 477/0 (incl. 2 new guard tests). [needs-user] device confirmation optional (the wart was self-recovering; the guards make it structurally impossible).
- **(T8.3.5 UX, user-reported 2026-06-16, NEW — internal-testing blocker) Cold-launch balance + progress flash.** On a fresh app start of an already-synced wallet: balance shows 0 then flips to X after a few seconds, and the smart widget flashes a low restore % (~0%) before dismissing. Once a wallet is mostly up to date, a few new blocks should NOT disrupt the UX — balance should show the last-known X immediately, and progress should read a truthful near-100% (e.g. 96.7%), never 0%. ROOT CAUSE (SDK regression vs old SDK): `SlipstreamSynchronizer` emits a COLD initial state — `prepare()` sends `accountsBalances=[:]` + `.disconnected` (SlipstreamSynchronizer.swift:218-221) and the first `.syncing` tick has `chainTip=0` → progress 0.0 with `[]` balance, until `cachedSummary` (nil at start, :157) + the first `GetLatestBlock` resolve. The OLD `SDKSynchronizer` is WARM: `snapshotState()` (:1242) loads `getAccountsBalances()` (real DB balance) + `latestBlocksDataProvider` (scanned/latest heights seeded from the DB on prepare, :177-178) on EVERY emission, so its first external emission already carries the real balance + a realistic progress. FIX (SDK, primary): warm the initial reporting — seed `prepare()`/first emission with the cached balance (`getWalletSummary`) + progress from the persisted `fullyScannedHeight` vs the persisted chain tip (near-100% on a synced wallet, never 0%), then refine when the live tip arrives. The 98% smart-widget visibility threshold + any last-balance display cache are Zodl-side (out of SDK scope; the SDK fix removes the root cause by emitting truthful values from the first emission). Mirrors the old SDK's `latestBlocksDataProvider`/`snapshotState` pattern. Named T8.3.5, slots before T8.4 (gates internal testing). **✅ SDK FIX SHIPPED 2026-06-16** (commit pending): pure-Swift — `prepare()` warm `initialState`, `start()` warm `.syncing`, tick floored by `max(counterProgress, summaryProgress)`; OfflineTests 483/0. Zodl-side follow-up (their repo, optional): confirm the 98% widget threshold + optionally a last-balance display cache; the SDK now emits truthful values so the flash should be gone without it. [needs-user] device confirm pending (Swift-only Zodl rebuild, no FFI).

- 2026-06-12 — L2 seam re-check (T6.8-L3a gate 4): CONFIRMED SEAM-BLOCKED. Inspected zcash_client_sqlite 0.21.0 `WalletDb::transactionally` (lib.rs:377-393): the closure receives `&mut WalletDb<SqlTransaction<'_>, &P, &CL, &mut R>` where `SqlTransaction` wraps `&rusqlite::Transaction<'conn>`. The `conn` field on `WalletDb` is PRIVATE (lib.rs:270: `conn: C`, no pub). No accessor method exists that exposes the inner `rusqlite::Transaction` or `rusqlite::Connection` to callers outside the crate. `Borrow<rusqlite::Transaction>` is implemented on `SqlTransaction` (lib.rs:287-290) but only for use in the same crate (where `conn.borrow()` is called directly). No `pub fn conn()`, no `pub fn raw_conn()`, no `as_rusqlite()`, no `inner()` — grepped all. Conclusion: **L2 confirmed seam-blocked; would require upstream patch to publicize the transaction handle, or a vendored fork — deferred.** Raw-SQL multi-row INSERT cannot be injected into the live transaction from outside the crate on current zcash_client_sqlite 0.21.

Replaced with a saturation-aware policy `lane_pool_policy()`: isolate (dedicated 2-thread pool) only when available_parallelism ≤ 4 (A10-class, where decrypt saturates the global pool — the T6.9 field pathology); share the global pool otherwise (≥6-core iPhone/Mac, their measured-best config). Heuristic honestly labeled: core count proxies "decrypt saturates the pool", which held in every measurement to date. Mac bench WITH policy: scan_s **5.80s** (regression gone; healthy 5.58-6.7 band). Both policy branches unit-tested (build_lane_pool factored). - 2026-06-13 — T6.9b DONE (dedicated lane thread pool — write-behind pool-queueing contention fix, #1755): **FIELD EVIDENCE** (from T6.9 iPad A10 run, 2026-06-13, stage split + per-chunk logs): persist_busy Σ=417.8s vs inline expected ~258s → 60% inflation; tree wall 12.9s vs pool-sum 6.7s on one chunk (chunk 3211166) = pool tasks queueing behind decrypt; depth-1 persist_wait 259.3s (vs theoretical ~0 with perfect overlap); net write-behind gain only ~20s vs ~120s projected. Cause: `rayon::join` in `sparse_put_blocks` + upstream `build_subtrees`' `par_chunks` ran on the GLOBAL rayon pool — the SAME pool the decrypt BatchRunners (zcash_client_backend rayon::spawn_fifo) saturate. On 4-core A10: two pools fighting for 4 cores; commit work queued behind decrypt tasks. **FIX (persist.rs, PersistLane)**: `LANE_POOL_THREADS=2` const (with doc comment explaining the 1-vs-2 tradeoff: 1=serial join, loses L3a intra-commit parallelism for ~0 benefit; 2=parallel join, preserve sapling∥orchard while giving decrypt its own 2 cores; on a 4-core device this is the right split); `Arc<rayon::ThreadPool>` created at `PersistLane::open()`; `submit_job` does `Arc::clone(&self.lane_pool)` and calls `lane_pool.install(|| job(&mut db, &mut sparse))` inside the `spawn_blocking` closure. Rayon scoping: `install` sets the thread as if it were a worker of `lane_pool` for the duration, so ALL nested rayon work (join+par_chunks) resolves to lane_pool workers. INLINE PATH (write_behind=false) UNTOUCHED. **HONEST PHYSICS NOTE**: total CPU is conserved on a saturated chip — this removes scheduling pathology (queueing/interleaving), not adds cores. The 5:00-5:30 iPad projection is labeled PROJECTION pending device re-test. **MAC BEFORE/AFTER** (50k, birthday=3325640, tip=3375640, zec.rocks, 2026-06-13): BEFORE (global pool, 2 runs): scan_s 5.61/5.55 (mean 5.58s), persist_wait 3.13/3.09 (mean 3.11s), persist_overlap 1.10/1.14 (mean 1.12s). AFTER (lane pool 2t, 4 runs): scan_s 11.02/12.35/10.82/10.72 (mean 11.23s), persist_wait 8.18/8.31/8.36/8.24 (mean 8.27s), persist_overlap 1.18/1.12/1.10/1.12 (mean 1.13s). Mac REGRESSION is expected and documented: Mac global pool has 10+ threads → commit ran fast via many threads; LANE_POOL_THREADS=2 limits commit parallelism on the many-core Mac but is the right budget for a 4-core device. Overlap (1.10-1.18s) unchanged — correct, since Mac was already persist-bound and overlap was capped by commit wall time. The device (A10 = 4 cores total, decrypt vs commit contending) is the meaningful test target. **MECHANISM** (file:line): `persist.rs` — `LANE_POOL_THREADS` const at top of "Persist lane" section; `PersistLane.lane_pool: Arc<rayon::ThreadPool>` field; `rayon::ThreadPoolBuilder::new().num_threads(LANE_POOL_THREADS).thread_name(…).build()` in `PersistLane::open()`; `Arc::clone(&self.lane_pool)` + `pool.install(|| job(…))` in `submit_job`. **TESTS**: +1 (`lane_pool_has_configured_thread_count` asserts `current_num_threads()==LANE_POOL_THREADS`); stress_sparse_join CLEAN (install in spawn_blocking changes pool for all nested work inside; no deadlock across all 3 variants). - 2026-06-12 — T6.9 L4b DONE (write-behind persistence pipelining, flag-gated default OFF, #1755): **THE RECON (read-surface enumeration, verbatim w/ citations)**: upstream `scan_cached_blocks` (zcash_client_backend-0.23.0 data_api/chain.rs:586-664) performs exactly FOUR DB reads + the put_blocks write: (1) `get_unified_full_viewing_keys` chain.rs:603-605 — never mutated by put_blocks → per-range cache, EXACT; (2) `block_metadata(from_height−1)` chain.rs:614-620 feeding check_hash_continuity (scanning/compact.rs:191-221; prev-height+prev-hash reorg detection — silently SKIPPED by upstream when the row is absent, so virtualizing it preserves boundary reorg detection that a stale/None read would LOSE) and PositionTracker::for_compact_block (compact.rs:400-516; prior tree sizes with chain_metadata fallback) → served from the pending unit's tail via ScannedBlock::to_block_metadata(), the IDENTICAL value upstream threads between blocks of one call (chain.rs:653); any other height = loud error; (3)+(4) `get_sapling_nullifiers(Unspent)`/`get_orchard_nullifiers(Unspent)` via Nullifiers::unspent (scanning.rs:360-368 ← chain.rs:623) → running unspent view: DB-seeded at range start under the no-pending barrier, advanced per stashed block with upstream's own update_with semantics (scanning.rs:435-464 — retain-spent-then-extend-found, per block in order; upstream RELIES on this view-equivalence for every multi-block call, we extend it one call boundary; SQLite Unspent semantics verified at zcash_client_sqlite-0.21.0 wallet/common.rs:155-189), re-seeded after every enhancement barrier (decrypt_and_store can add/spend notes outside put_blocks). Known benign residue (documented in code): a note arriving already-spent via nullifier_map detection stays in the in-memory view exactly as it does in upstream's own intra-call view — unobservable (a nullifier can appear at most once per chain) and empirically covered by the byte-identity oracles. NOTHING else is read — every other facade method fails loudly, so upstream read-surface drift = loud test failure, never a silent stale read. **DESIGN**: `WriteBehindFacade` (persist.rs; no DB handle at all — the 4 reads come from seeded+rolled state; put_blocks stashes (from_state.clone(), blocks) into a depth-1 slot with an occupied-stash guard) + `PersistLane` (persist.rs; owns a SECOND WalletDb connection to the same WAL file + the per-range SparseTreeState; jobs are FnOnce(&mut Db, &mut SparseTreeState) boxed closures so lane mechanics are hermetically testable without ScannedBlock [not publicly constructible — T6.0 fact]; production job = the EXACT sparse_put_blocks call, rows+tree+flush in ONE transactionally per unit; ownership of (Db, SparseTreeState) ping-pongs through spawn_blocking so strict serialization is STRUCTURAL; submit(N+1) awaits N first and propagates its error BEFORE spawning — depth-1 backpressure; a panic loses the lane connection → loud error, pass fails, wallet file consistent via SQLite rollback-on-drop). TREE-STATE OWNERSHIP CHOICE (controller question answered): the per-chunk tree work moved ENTIRELY into the deferred commit closure — state owned by the persist lane, scan side never touches it; insert+flush remain in the same transaction (crash-consistency unchanged from T6.5 analysis). No-concurrent-writer argument: while a commit runs, the scan side does ZERO DB work (reads virtualized); enhancement/reorg-truncate/suggest/summary run only behind `drain()` barriers (range end, all error exits via wrapper-around-inner extraction so `?` lands at the barrier, interleave points). Error precedence on double failure: persist error wins over ScanContinuity (DB-integrity signal > recoverable reorg; reorg retried next pass) — documented in scan.rs. scan_chunks_from_treestate gained the same (sparse, write_behind) params for darkside (single-chunk only — v0.4.9 synthesized treestates can't split tx-bearing ranges; REAL multi-boundary coverage lives in the hermetic + mainnet oracles). FFI: zero changes — EngineConfig::new defaults write_behind=false at rust/src/lib.rs:4490; device A/B = one-line flip + rebuild (NEXT ACTION). **INSTRUMENTATION**: per-await `write-behind persist awaited` line (persist_wait_ms + persist_busy_ms + unit span); chunk log gains persist_wait_ms; stage-split gains persist_wait_s + persist_overlap_s (busy−wait clamped ≥0) + write_behind field; scan_s deliberately KEEPS wait (honest loop wall — comment in engine.rs); rows/tree/flush lines unchanged inside the deferred commit. **TESTS**: +24 (core 118→138+1ign: 3 nullifier-delta incl. THE pending-spend case, 6 facade incl. loud-unvirtualized + depth-1 stash guard + known-absent None, 5 lane incl. backpressure/error-before-next-submit/drain-propagates/panic-poisons, 2 hermetic WB oracles, 2 config, +3 scheduler/default-fields; cli 19→23: bare-flag/explicit-false/default-off/--write-behind-b). **GATES (all green)**: cargo 138+1ign core + 23 cli + 1 stress; clippy ZERO both feature sets; hermetic WB oracles CLEAN (write_behind_matches_upstream_on_synthetic_chain 3×1000 + write_behind_matches_upstream_on_dense_split_chain at REAL ChunkSplitter boundaries); - 2026-06-12 — T6.8-H2 DONE (transient-error retries — scan-path treestate + bounded pass retry, #1755): **FIELD EVIDENCE (user, iPhone 16 Pro, ENGINE_BUILD=2026-06-13.sandblast-split build)**: chunk 3361166 scanned fine in 3.77s; then `zcashlc: slipstream sync failed err=transport: get_tree_state: timed out after 30s` → whole sync dead, UI stuck at 6.1%. The B2 unary deadline (hardening1) correctly converted a formerly-infinite hang into a fast error — but the scan-side treestate calls had ZERO retries, so one 30s server stall was FATAL. Same servers produced 3 simultaneous stream stalls in another run today (recovered via the fetch path's retries) — server-side stalls are common. **FIX A (scan-path treestate retries, grpc.rs + scan.rs)**: added `retry_get_tree_state(endpoint, height, context)` helper in grpc.rs — up to TREESTATE_RETRY_MAX=2 additional attempts (3 total); backoff schedule: attempt 1 = 1s, attempt 2 = 3s; RECONNECT on every attempt (fresh grpc::connect avoids reusing a wedged channel); non-transient errors (decode/proto/logic) propagate immediately; warn! with attempt+context+height+backoff_ms per retry. Applied to: (1) initial treestate seed in scan_chunks at scan.rs:169 (was `grpc::get_tree_state(client, range_start-1)` → now `grpc::retry_get_tree_state(&endpoint, range_start-1, "initial seed")`); (2) per-chunk-boundary prefetch spawn at scan.rs:222–229 — retry INSIDE the spawned task so the scan/fetch overlap is preserved (was `grpc::get_tree_state(&mut c, sub_end)` → now `grpc::retry_get_tree_state(&ep, sub_end, "chunk-boundary prefetch")`). Enhancement-path unary GETs already non-fatal (non-linear; enhancement errors warn+continue) — skip. **FIX B (bounded pass-level retry, rust/src/lib.rs)**: module-level constants PASS_RETRY_MAX=2 (doc: total 3 attempts), pass_retry_sleep(attempt) (1→5s, 2+→15s); pure function `should_retry(err, attempt) → Option<Duration>` (returns Some(sleep) if err.is_transient() && attempt <= PASS_RETRY_MAX, else None; extracted for unit tests). sync_body now wraps `sync_once` in a `loop { … break Ok/Err }` retry loop: increment attempt on Err; call should_retry; if Some → warn! + tokio::time::sleep + continue Syncing; if None → break Err (exhausted or non-transient). SyncStarted (tag=1) emitted ONCE before the loop. SyncState stays Syncing throughout retries. **Progress %-restart semantics (noted for UX)**: begin_pass() at each sync_once entry resets ratio counters (scanned/fetched/pass_total/range_end/spendable) — % briefly resets to 0 over the REMAINING work; correct because re-suggest returns remaining ranges; monotonic delta counters (enhanced_txs/ranges_completed/reorgs_recovered) untouched across retries. Acceptable UX for a rare 30s-server-stall recovery. **is_transient classification** (SlipstreamError::is_transient, error.rs): Transport=true (timeout/connect/stream stall — retry after reconnect); Config=false (bad config; not fixable by retry); Wallet=false (DB/scan error; would repeat); ScanContinuity=false (internally handled by reorg arm); Discontinuity=false (fetch continuity bug); MisbehavingServer=false (bad proto/decode); Stopped=false (lifecycle). **TESTS**: is_transient exhaustive 7-variant (error.rs tests::*_is_{not_}transient); grpc::tests: retry_constants_are_sane, transport_error_is_transient_for_retry, retry_treestate_succeeds_on_first_attempt; lib.rs slipstream_retry_tests (5 tests): should_retry_transient_attempt_1_returns_5s, should_retry_transient_attempt_2_returns_15s, should_retry_transient_exhausted_returns_none, should_retry_non_transient_returns_none_always, pass_retry_max_is_two. **GATES**: cargo 118+1ign core + 19 cli + 1 stress (117 slipstream-core + 1 stress; 5 new lib.rs tests run separately); clippy ZERO both feature sets (pre-existing `manual_is_multiple_of` in voting/vote.rs unchanged); hermetic oracles 7/7 CLEAN; darkside serial 10/10 (live_lightd_info_smoke, sparse_pipeline_matches_upstream_on_darkside_fixture, truncate_rescan_oracle_is_clean, reorg_recovery_produces_correct_tip, reorg_recovery_produces_correct_tip_sparse, fixture_funds_are_spendable_after_full_sync, sync_enhancement_stores_raw_fields, sync_finds_fixture_transactions, parallel_fetch_5000_blocks_in_order, stage_apply_and_read_tip_roundtrip); mainnet tip−2000 oracle (birthday=3373336, tip=3375336, --sparse-b) VERDICT IDENTICAL (26 tables OK); ENGINE_BUILD → "2026-06-13.retry1"; init-local-ffi.sh full 3 slices + tag freshness probe (ios-arm64=1, ios-sim=2, macos=2); OfflineTests 475/0 SPM-purged. - 2026-06-12 — T6.8-S DONE (byte-budgeted adaptive chunk splitting — sandblasting era traversable, #1755): **FIELD EVIDENCE (user, old wallet birthday 935000, Mac-class device, previous day's pre-hardening build)**: normal era 10k chunks ≈ 6-9k outputs, scan ~1s; at 1,700,001+ outputs explode — 346,271 then 1,237,313 then 2,536,689 per 10k chunk (scan 21s/69s/44s) — and fetch broke down completely: `chunk fetch timed out; retrying worker_id=N index=80-84 attempt=1/2/3...` looping forever (a sandblasting 10k chunk is ~hundreds of MB wire; it cannot complete within the 120s whole-chunk timeout and each retry restarted the WHOLE giant chunk; single-chunk RAM ≈300-500 MB → jetsam). User mandate: make 1.7M→2.0M traversable ("instead of 10k batch use 1k or even 500"). **DESIGN (density-adaptive, NO hardcoded heights)**: (1) `EngineConfig.chunk_split_bytes` (default 8 MiB = DEFAULT_CHUNK_SPLIT_BYTES, validate ≥1 MiB, CLI `--chunk-split-bytes`); (2) fetch workers stream their plan chunk through `ChunkSplitter` (emit-before-add; a single oversized block forms its own sub-chunk; yielded sub-chunks never empty, order-preserving) and emit `SubChunk { plan_index, sub_index, is_last, bytes, blocks, permit }`; (3) reorder stage `release_ordered()` keys on (plan_index, sub_index), releases plan N's subs strictly in order, advances to N+1 only after N's is_last; Chunk.index stays dense 0,1,2…; per-plan split summary logged at info (`plan chunk split into sub-chunks`); (4) **DEADLINE-RESET**: `FetchPlan.chunk_timeout` (120s) re-scoped to ONE sub-chunk's accumulation, reset after every emission (admission/backpressure time excluded by reset placement) — a healthy-but-huge chunk can NEVER time out; a truly stalled stream still dies via the 30s B2 stream-idle deadline; (5) **RESUME-FROM-HEIGHT RETRY**: PlanChunkCursor tracks resume_from = last emitted sub-chunk end + 1; a retry re-opens GetBlockRange AT resume_from (already-released heights are never re-sent → double-release/double-scan structurally impossible); sub_index continues across attempts (dense for the emitter); `retries_per_chunk` re-defined as consecutive ZERO-PROGRESS attempts (an attempt that emitted ≥1 sub-chunk resets the budget — total attempts bounded by subs×retries); clean-but-SHORT stream end = retryable Transport error (never silent block loss, never an empty chunk to scan — scan errors on empty chunks); (6) **AheadGate (necessary addition discovered in recon)**: without it, K−1 non-floor workers each streaming a ~0.3-1 GB plan chunk would park everything in the unbounded reorder `pending` map (4 streams ≈ 1-3 GB → jetsam — a latent pre-existing hole that splitting alone makes WORSE); now non-floor sub-chunks acquire ahead-budget permits (8×split_bytes = 64 MiB default) released when the emitter hands them to the ChunkQueue; the floor plan chunk is EXEMPT (always streams), waiters re-check the floor every 100ms (deadlock-free: floor never waits on budget; held budget drains exactly when its subs become the floor); side effect: a budget-paused stream may be EOF-cut by the server's LB — absorbed cleanly by resume (observed live, below); (7) scan side UNCHANGED — from_state threading keys off actual chunk heights (verified at scan.rs:228-245 invariants; treestate prefetch per sub-chunk end; per-chunk put_blocks commits just happen more often in dense eras — desirable); ChunkQueue budget + clamp path untouched. **UPSTREAM FINDING (dense-oracle construction)**: chunk boundaries ARE observable in the wallet DB on the upstream path itself, by design — put_blocks prunes `nullifier_map` at fully-scanned−PRUNING_DEPTH on EVERY call (zcash_client_backend-0.23.0 data_api/ll/wallet.rs:460-463 → zcash_client_sqlite-0.21.0 lib.rs:2054) and shard-blob bytes encode insert/prune batch history → cross-CHUNKING byte-comparison is upstream-false; the oracle compares the two PATHS at EQUAL boundaries (T6.2+ methodology; CLI oracle unaffected — both runs share one config). **TESTS (+20: core 107+1ign, cli 19)**: splitter 4 (threshold split order-preserving, small-blocks fast path, oversized-block own sub-chunk, empty finish); pump 6 (ordered subs + single final is_last, single-sub fast path identical to today, short-stream errors without emitting tail, RESUME-after-midstream-error → no duplicate heights + dense sub_index across the retry boundary, deadline RESETS on each emission [start_paused: 30s slow stream vs 5s deadline completes], deadline FIRES without progress); AheadGate 2 (floor exempt under exhausted budget; blocked ahead worker unblocks when it becomes floor); release_ordered 3 (scrambled multi-worker arrival → strict height order + dense Chunk.index + floor advance; retry-continuation interleaving; cross-sub-chunk discontinuity rejected); config 2 (≥1 MiB validation); CLI 2 (default + override); dense hermetic oracle `split_chunking_matches_upstream_on_dense_chain` (80 blk × 20 outs ≈ 2.5 KB/blk vs 6 KiB split → 40 windows ≥30 via the REAL ChunkSplitter; H3 closed: .synced emits immediately with cached balances, post-Done summary refreshes on later ticks (poll loop continues) — ordering fine, values were just masked.
