- 2026-06-12 — T6.8-L1 DONE (Zodl, commit 74c81024): race-servers-BEFORE-first-start. RECON MAP: (1) Race trigger — `AutoServerSelectionClient.liveValue.refreshIfEnabled` (AutoServerSelectionLiveKey.swift:11-51): calls `sdkSynchronizer.evaluateBestOf(endpoints, 5.0s, 1 block, 3 candidates, network)` then `transactionGuard.switchIfIdle { sdkSynchronizer.switchToEndpoint(best) }`. (2) When triggered (BEFORE fix) — AFTER `start()`: `initializeSDK` (RootInitialization.swift:354 `start(false)`) sends `.initializationSuccessfullyDone` which merges `.refreshAutomaticServer` (RootStore.swift:406); `retryStart` (RootInitialization.swift:194 `start(true)`) sends `.refreshAutomaticServer` (line 200). `.refreshAutomaticServer` handler (RootStore.swift:430-439) calls `await autoServerSelection.refreshIfEnabled()` in a `.run`. (3) switchTo application — `AutoServerSelectionLiveKey.swift:39-43` `transactionGuard.switchIfIdle { withTimeout(60s) { sdkSynchronizer.switchToEndpoint(best) } }` → `SlipstreamSynchronizer.switchTo` → F3 warning when wasRunning==true. FIX: added `await autoServerSelection.refreshIfEnabled()` BEFORE `start()` in both paths: `initializeSDK` (before line 354 `start(false)`) and `retryStart` (before line 194 `start(true)`, gated on `state.bgTask == nil`). Pre-start `switchTo` is a cheap no-op: `wasRunning==false` → no restart, `engine.reopen` just opens a new handle. MANUAL-MODE PROOF: `refreshIfEnabled` first guard `guard userStoredPreferences.automaticServerSelection() == true else { return }` (AutoServerSelectionLiveKey.swift:17) — function returns immediately when automatic mode is off; manual server choice is never touched. TIMEOUT: `evaluateBestOf` is bounded by `evaluationTimeoutSeconds=5.0` (AutoServerSelectionConstants; passed as `fetchThresholdSeconds` stream break) plus fast gRPC `try?` calls — no new unbounded wait. bgTask guard in retryStart matches existing `.refreshAutomaticServer` handler (RootStore.swift:435). Post-start `.refreshAutomaticServer` retained for mid-session re-selection. Build: zodl-internal iOS Simulator BUILD SUCCEEDED. No unit tests for the Root sequencing change (none existed). Zodl commit: 74c81024; branch slipstream; Zodl git status clean. SDK repo: no changes.

- 2026-06-11 — init-local-ffi.sh nesting bug FOUND + FIXED (root cause of BOTH framework corruptions this session): the install step `cp -R built.xcframework LocalPackages/libzcashlc.xcframework` nests the new framework INSIDE an existing destination (BSD cp -R semantics), leaving stale top-level slices that Xcode silently uses. The T5.6 agent's "corruption" was this script bug, not agent error. Fix: `rm -rf "$XCFRAMEWORK_DIR"` before install in both paths (source-build + --cached unzip). Containment note: Scripts/init-local-ffi.sh edit is a deliberate exception to D1 (local-dev tooling the slipstream workflow + the user both depend on; two corruption incidents in one session; rebuild-local-ffi.sh already used the correct rm+mv swap pattern). Final framework state verified per-slice: canonical layout (Info.plist + 3 slices at top level), ranges_completed in all headers, eb7711a9 code in all binaries (strings probe: 'per-range enhancement failed'), 6 slipstream symbols each. Landmine documented in CONVENTIONS.
- 2026-06-11 — T5.6 review fixes (controller, follow-up commit on top of 3d7bf613): (1) FOUND framework corruption: the T5.6 agent left LocalPackages/libzcashlc.xcframework with a full xcframework NESTED inside the old one + only a macOS slice at top level (device builds would be stale/broken) → clean `rm -rf LocalPackages` + full init-local-ffi.sh; canonical layout restored (Info.plist + 3 slices at top level, ranges_completed verified in each header). (2) HARDENING per-range enhancement: the agent's F3 block used fatal `?` on grpc::connect + run_enhancement — a transient network blip at a range boundary would abort a multi-minute sync (single-shot runner → SyncState::Error until app-lifecycle retry). Now non-fatal: warn + continue; final post-loop run remains the correctness backstop. (3) FOUND latent cross-pass counter staleness (predates T5.6, latent since T5.5): Swift prepare() opens the handle ONCE; stop()/start() reuse it, so Progress atomics survive passes — a backgrounded+resumed restore would compute scanned(stale)/pass_total(remaining) → 59% at start, pinned 100% later. Fix: Progress::begin_pass() called at sync_once entry resets RATIO counters (scanned/fetched/pass_total/range_end/spendable latch); monotonic delta counters (enhanced_txs, ranges_completed, reorgs) deliberately preserved — Swift consumes them via strict-greater last-seen comparisons. (4) clippy regressions fixed (collapsible if → let-chain; SyncReport struct-init in test helper; MAX_CONSECUTIVE_REORGS bounds moved to compile-time `const _: ()` assertions; stray `u8 as u8` in verify.rs — last two surfaced by a newer clippy on old code). VERIFIED: cargo 64+14/0, clippy 0 warnings BOTH feature sets, darkside 6/6 (incl. reorg_recovery_produces_correct_tip through the new per-range-enhancement scheduler), OfflineTests green after SPM purge.

Swift resets lastRangesCompleted on handle destroy/create. Tests +4 Rust +5 Swift.
- 2026-06-11 — T5.5 done: counter-based sync progress + spendable hint; summary calls fully eliminated during scan; sub-batching made opt-in (None default). ROOT CAUSE CORRECTION: the T5.3 per-commit estimate (1.5–2s fixed on A10) is RETRACTED — device log shows single 10k batch = 38.1s/26.5k outputs vs 10×1k batches = 39.6s/28.9k outputs (≈neutral; no commit overhead penalty at this scale). The REAL regression was the summary-parasite: every 8s a 10–30s shard walk ran concurrently with scanning on a 4-core A10 → 20–35% per-output CPU theft + SQLite contention. FIX: during state==1 (Syncing), ZERO getWalletSummary calls — progress from engine counters (scannedBlocks/passTotalBlocks), spendability from spendableHint latch. RUST: Progress gains pass_total_blocks (AtomicU64, accumulates range block-lengths as scheduler takes them) + spendable_hint (AtomicU64 latch, set when ChainTip-priority range completes); accessors add_pass_total(n)/set_spendable() + read pass_total()/spendable(); both exposed in FfiSlipstreamSnapshot (ffi_handle.rs + lib.rs twins, appended at END for padding stability); snapshot() populates; header regenerated (3 slices, pass_total_blocks u64 + spendable_hint u8 verified in each). SUB-BATCHING: scan_batch_target_ms=None (default) → single batch per chunk; Some(ms) → adaptive sub-batching (neutral per A10 evidence but opt-in retained for future investigation). SWIFT: SlipstreamSnapshot gains passTotalBlocks:UInt64 + spendableHint:UInt8 (memberwise init default=0 for backward compat); counterProgress(scanned:total:) pure static helper (min(Float(scanned)/Float(max(total,1)), 1.0)); tickPoll state==1 path uses counter progress + spendableHint, NO kickSummaryFetchIfNeeded; kickSummaryFetchIfNeeded returns early for state==1; state==3 (Done) emits .synced immediately then kicks ONE post-sync summary fetch; states 0/2 summary at 2s cadence unchanged. SUMMARY INTERVAL: summaryFetchInterval(forState:) returns 2s for ALL states (8s Syncing branch removed — superseded by full elimination); testSummaryIntervalWhileSyncingIs8s test REPLACED with testSummaryIntervalWhileSyncingIs2sNotEight. SECONDARY OBSERVATIONS RECORDED: (1) per-1k-batch times escalate through run (3.5s→6-7s) — WAL/checkpoint pressure grows; T6 evidence; (2) AutoServerSelection restarted pass once (zec.rocks→eu.zec.rocks, ~15s cost; F2 same-endpoint guard can't help — different endpoint; acceptable); (3) Zodl emitted `synchronizerStartFailed(.rustSlipstreamNotOpen)` at app launch before prepare (self-recovered; recorded as named follow-up wart in Blockers). TESTS: cargo: 60/1ign (+3: progress_pass_total_and_spendable_hint, counter_progress_ratio, ffi_snapshot_counter_fields_roundtrip); OfflineTests: 458/0 (+8 vs 450: testSummaryIntervalWhileSyncingIs2sNotEight + 6 counterProgress tests + 2 snapshot-new-fields tests). REBUILD: full 3-slice XCFramework via init-local-ffi.sh; pass_total_blocks + spendable_hint verified in all 3 headers; 6 symbols per slice confirmed. COMMIT: [#1755] slipstream: counter-based sync progress + spendable hint; summary calls eliminated during scan; sub-batching opt-in.

- 2026-06-11 — T4.9 postscript (user clarification): the "minutes vs 10s" slowdown was HARDWARE, not a regression — the test device was an old iPad where 10k-block chunks grind (scan-bound). The frozen-%-jumps were still our real bug (T4.8 poll awaiting the 10–30s getWalletSummary) — T4.9 fix stands. LESSONS → P5: (1) device-adaptive chunk sizing (target ~few-seconds per chunk: smaller chunks on weak hardware = smoother progress, finer commits, lower memory; config.chunk_blocks is already a tunable — P5 auto-tunes from measured chunk wall-clock); (2) progress VALUE advances per chunk commit by nature — smaller chunks also smooth the bar; (3) old-iPad class belongs in the G5 device matrix; (4) upstream note recorded: get_wallet_summary needs a progress-only fast path (P5/P6 conversation).
- 2026-06-11 — FORMAL DEVICE MATRIX (user-measured, ground truth; supersedes the informal 10.8× single run). Wallet: 8-month, 286,855 blocks. iPad 6th Gen (A1893/A10): Slipstream 16:04 vs old SDK 1:04:26 = 4.01×; Zingo 11:34 (1.39× faster than us); Zkool 8:53 (1.81× faster than us). iPhone 16 Pro: Slipstream 3:28 vs old SDK 16:55 = 4.88×. ANALYSIS: (1) vs old SDK: 4.0–4.9× formal (earlier informal run 10.8× — server/load variance; both recorded). G2 ≥5× criterion: borderline at formal conditions — honest wording applied to gate row. (2) G5 extrapolation: iPhone 16 Pro ≈ 725s/1M ≈ 12 min ≤ 15-min target — plausible, formal 1M device run pending. (3) **P6 ENTRY CONDITION MET**: on CPU-bound hardware we lose to warp-lineage engines (Zkool=hanh's warp 1.81×, Zingo 1.39×) — exactly the SLIPSTREAM_DESIGN §P6 hypothesis (upstream scanner + SQLite shardtree vs warp frontier math). Sequence: P5 instrumentation first (quantify scan-vs-commit-vs-summary share of the iPad's 16:04; adaptive chunks may claw back part), then P6 kernel + sparse tree for the CPU-bound gap.
- 2026-06-11 — DEVICE MATRIX COMPLETED (user): iPhone 16 Pro — Zingo 2:41 (161s, 1.29× faster than us), Zkool 1:13 (73s, 2.85× faster than us) vs our 3:28 (208s). CONTROLLER'S PRIOR HYPOTHESIS FALSIFIED: we do NOT win on modern hardware; Zkool's lead GROWS with fast silicon (1.81×→2.85×). Implication: the scan core (upstream scan_cached_blocks + per-chunk serial commit + SQLite shardtree) is our ceiling on ALL hardware, not just old devices — fetch is a solved problem (~10s of the 208s by G1-era rates), so ~190s is scan-side. Zkool@73s ≈ near the design-doc floor → kernel-class scanning is achievable on-device. P6 GOAL LINE (recorded): beat 73s on iPhone 16 Pro and 533s on iPad 6th Gen for this 286,855-block wallet. SEQUENCE CONFIRMED: T5.0 instrumentation FIRST (decompose the 208s: decrypt vs put_blocks commit vs treestate vs enhance — engine logs already carry per-range elapsed; FFI snapshot to surface stage split on-device), then P6 kernel v2 + sparse tree + parallel chunk decrypt with ordered serial commit.
- 2026-06-11 — ROADMAP AMENDMENT (user decision): P7 Android DECOUPLED from the prototype. Rationale: zcash-android-sdk already exists with its own Rust-FFI backend (backend-lib) mirroring the iOS SDK's architecture — shipping a uniffi blackbox module would not be adopted. Strategy: iOS = reference implementation, delivered to the highest bar; Android later ports by reference (slipstream-core is platform-neutral by construction and can slot into their backend the way it slotted into libzcashlc). M2 deferred; no prototype gate depends on Android.
- 2026-06-11 — iPhone 16 Pro LOG DECOMPOSITION (user log, 269,070 blocks, ~208s wall): fetch 8s TOTAL (5s+3s, overlapped→hidden); setup ~2-4s; enhancement ~1-2s; **scan ≈195s = 28 chunks ≈ 7s per 10k chunk**. Density: 168MB/269k ≈ 625B/blk ≈ ~3.5 outputs/blk → ~35k outputs/chunk. Trial-decryption floor on A18 ≈ 0.15-0.3s/chunk → **decryption is ~3-5% of scan; ~95% is the upstream scanner's persistence path** (shardtree-through-SQLite insertion of ~900k commitments, 10k blocks-table rows/chunk, nullifier upserts, decode, key prep, commit). Zkool cross-check: 73s total ≈ 55-60s scan ≈ 4,700 blk/s vs our 1,380 — consistent with frontier+owned-notes-only tree math (design doc §3.3). **P6 FORK SETTLED: sparse tree/persistence replacement LEADS; decrypt kernel is secondary (~5-10s of 195).** T5.0 first deliverable: per-chunk elapsed in the scan tracing line + engine-end stage-split log (current chunk lines carry no timing — the 7s/chunk is derived). - 2026-06-11 — T6.5 COMPATIBILITY ARGUMENT (verbatim from plan §D / §6.5, record as instructed): "checkpoint cadence is inherited, not redesigned — `scan_block` emits per-block `Retention::Checkpoint` markers (scanning/compact.rs:672-810); our in-memory ShardTree ingests them through upstream's own `insert_tree`/`prune_excess_checkpoints` (max 100, = PRUNING_DEPTH); the flush materializes the surviving checkpoints into the same `{pool}_tree_checkpoints` tables. `truncate_to_height` → `select_truncation_height` (wallet.rs:3639-3668) therefore finds the same MAX(height ≤ requested ∧ in blocks ∧ in checkpoints) and `ScanContinuity{at}` arithmetic (`rewind = at.saturating_sub(10)`, scheduler.rs:209) is untouched. SparseTreeState is per-range (created in scan_chunks, dropped on return) so a truncate between ranges can never observe stale in-memory tree state; within a range, an error abandons the un-flushed delta — the DB remains exactly at the last committed chunk, which is the same crash/abort contract as upstream's per-call `transactionally`." Empirical gate: darkside suite 9 tests all green; truncate+rescan oracle CLEAN; reorg sparse variant asserts identical ScanContinuity/truncate/tip as upstream.

- 2026-06-16 — T8.3.5 done: warm fresh-start reporting (NEW UX regression the user reported during T8.x — cold app launch flashed balance 0→X and the smart widget ~0%→hidden on an already-synced wallet). Pure-Swift SDK fix, NO Rust/FFI change. Root cause: `SlipstreamSynchronizer` emitted a COLD initial state — `prepare()`→`[:]`/`.disconnected`, `start()`→`.syncing(0,false)`, first tick→PASS-LOCAL `counterProgress` (0% of the few new blocks on a catch-up) — until `cachedSummary` + the live tip resolved. The old SDK was WARM (snapshotState loads getAccountsBalances + latestBlocksDataProvider on every emission). FIX (3 parts via new pure helpers in `SlipstreamSynchronizer+PureHelpers.swift`): (1) `prepare()` seeds `cachedSummary = getWalletSummary()` + emits `initialState(from:syncSessionID:)` — WARM (real balance + `summaryProgress` from the summary's scanProgress) when a summary exists, COLD `.disconnected` only for a genuinely fresh wallet; (2) `start()` seeds its initial `.syncing` from `cachedSummary` instead of `(0,false)`; (3) the `.syncing` tick replaces `counterProgress` with `syncingProgress = max(counterProgress, summaryProgress(cachedSummary))` — FLOORS the pass-local counter with the wallet's GLOBAL progress, so a small catch-up reads ~100% (widget hidden via Zodl's >98% rule) while a from-birthday restore (summary ≈ 0 at start) still climbs 0→100%, and a resumed restore no longer restarts at 0%. New helpers `summaryProgress`/`syncingProgress`/`initialState` + 6 OfflineTests (summaryProgress(nil)=0, restore-uses-counter, catch-up-floored, live-dominates-stale, initialState cold/warm). GATES: `swift test --filter OfflineTests` **483/0** (477+6); SwiftLint zero NEW violations — kept `SlipstreamSynchronizer.swift` under the 600-line cap by extracting `initialState` to the helpers file, and switched the 3 syncingProgress asserts to extract-then-assert to avoid `multiline_arguments`. SDK/ZODL BOUNDARY: the 98% smart-widget visibility threshold + any last-balance display cache live in Zodl (their repo); the SDK now emits truthful balance + progress from the first emission, so Zodl's existing logic hides the widget without further SDK work. - 2026-06-16 — T8.3.6 done (POST-P8 tweak from device testing): the incoming mempool tx showed "received" (terminal) instead of "receiving" (pending). ROOT CAUSE (investigated via Explore + direct reads, not guessed): `SlipstreamSynchronizer` never populated `ZcashTransaction.Overview.state`. `transactionRepository.find` leaves `state == nil` (it's an optional set POST-fetch from the current chain height — TransactionEntity.swift:66, init(row:) at :178-225 doesn't set it); unlike `SDKSynchronizer`, which wraps every fetch in `enhanceRawTransactionsWithState` → `getState(for: latestKnownBlockHeight)` (SDKSynchronizer.swift:566/580), the Slipstream accessors returned raw txs. Zashi's `TransactionState.init` maps an INCOMING tx via `let isPending = isSentTransaction ? minedHeight==nil : transaction.state == .pending` → `nil == .pending` → false → `.received` (secant TransactionState.swift:401, 413-414). Invisible on confirmed receives (they're "received" anyway), WRONG on the 0-conf mempool tx. NOT a mined_height issue — Deviation D1's `None` is fine: `minedHeight==nil` → SDK State `.pending` (TransactionEntity.swift:38) → "receiving" ONCE state is populated, so NO D1 revert. FIX (pure Swift, NO FFI): `SlipstreamSynchronizer.enhanceWithState(_:)` resolves the current chain height (`latestState.latestBlockHeight`, fallback `rustBackend.maxScannedHeight`) and applies the new pure `SlipstreamSynchronizer.transactionsWithState(_:currentHeight:)` (+PureHelpers — maps `Overview.getState` over each tx); wired into `allTransactions`/`allTransactions(from:)`/`allSentTransactions`/`allReceivedTransactions` + BOTH `foundTransactions` emissions (the live new-tx event). Mirrors SDKSynchronizer parity. GATES: `swift test --filter OfflineTests` 483/0 (no regression); SwiftLint clean — kept `SlipstreamSynchronizer.swift` ≤600 lines by extracting the map to the pure helper (the lone line_length:1053 warning is pre-existing). Not separately unit-tested (Overview has no test-friendly init; parity-by-construction + the [needs-user] re-test is the validation). [needs-user] re-test: Swift-only Zodl rebuild (NO FFI — reuse the t84-devmem slice) → the incoming mempool tx now shows "receiving"/pending, settling to "received" after confirmations.
- 2026-06-16 — T8.7 done (POST-P8 resilience, from device testing): a tester hit `syncStatus: .error(.rustSlipstreamSyncFailed(0))` / `[ZRUST0096] sync pass failed during a polling tick`. ROOT CAUSE: the `(0)` payload = `snap.chainTip` at failure; `begin_pass` does NOT reset `chain_tip` (events.rs:143-150) and `set_chain_tip` runs only after the tip is fetched (engine.rs:187), so `chainTip==0` ⇒ the tip was NEVER set ⇒ the INITIAL sync pass failed at PREFLIGHT (connect / GetLatestBlock — transient network) across all of `run_pass_with_retry`'s attempts, then `Error(1)`; the Swift tickPoll observed state==2 → `.error(.rustSlipstreamSyncFailed(0))`. PRINCIPLE: a recoverable sync failure must NEVER surface a hard `.error` to a tester. FIX (rust/src/lib.rs `sync_body`, FFI): (1) the INITIAL pass is wrapped in a RESILIENT retry loop — a TRANSIENT (`err.is_transient()`) failure logs (warn) + sets `Idle` (Swift `.disconnected`) + sleeps a capped-exponential backoff (`sync_retry_backoff`: 3/6/12/24/30s) + retries FOREVER (never gives up on the network); only a NON-transient (config/logic) error still surfaces `Error` (a real, non-recoverable problem); panics still → `Error(2)` via the B1 supervisor. (2) the FOLLOW loop NO LONGER surfaces `Error` on ANY path — transient + non-transient probe failures and catch-up-pass failures all log + retry, staying synced to `last_tip` (a catch-up failure reverts to `Done`, not `Error`); `FOLLOW_FAILURE_CAP` repurposed from "cap before Error" → an escalated-logging threshold (no Error). So the ONLY hard-`.error` paths left: the initial pass's NON-transient errors + panics. New pure `sync_retry_backoff(attempt)` (capped exp, overflow-safe) + unit test; FOLLOW_FAILURE_CAP doc updated. ENGINE_BUILD → `2026-06-16.t87-resilient-sync`. GATES: libzcashlc compiles + clippy clean (only the pre-existing `is_multiple_of`); retry-tests 12/12 (incl. `sync_retry_backoff_grows_then_caps`; follow/mempool cap-sane still hold); slipstream-core 144+1ign / cli 29; full `init-local-ffi.sh` (3 slices, new sync_body); OfflineTests [pending the rebuild]. The os_log `err=…` line would confirm the exact failing RPC (almost certainly a transient connect/GetLatestBlock at sync start), but the resilience covers it regardless. [needs-user] device re-test (rebuild Zodl, tag `t87-resilient-sync`): a transient network blip at sync start now shows `.disconnected` + retries (NO `.error`/`rustSlipstreamSyncFailed`); a synced wallet never drops to error from a follow blip.
- 2026-06-17 — zallet↔slipstream sync-engine comparison (answering the core-team question "why build slipstream instead of using zallet?"). Ran an 8-phase adversarial workflow (17 agents, ~1.5M tok) → **`docs/slipstream/2026-06-17-zallet-vs-slipstream.md`**. VERDICT: **#3 MERGE, slipstream-foundation.** zallet is a desktop full-node daemon (in-process Zaino indexer + co-located Zebra/zcashd + FULL blocks + no memory budget + crash-on-error→systemd) → structurally disqualified as a mobile drop-in; slipstream wins/ties every phase *for mobile* and loses none. Two PORTABLE wins to steal from zallet: ① a **persistent decrypt-AHEAD engine** (slipstream rebuilds its `BatchRunner` per `scan_cached_blocks` call), ② **inline surgical reorg**. HONEST CORE: slipstream's decrypt **kernel** is the *identical upstream BatchRunner on the same rayon pool* as zallet — the NEON/GPU kernel is aspirational/parked — so the decrypt edge today is **payload (compact 52B vs full 580B) + overlap, not a faster inner loop**. **VERIFIED POST-HOC (better-to-know):** the workflow's "fix the internal docs that mis-credit the persistence win to shard I/O" did **NOT hold** — STATE T6.3/T6.3b + perf-learnings + book ch.7 already correctly attribute the win to the **T6.3b checkpoint-downgrade (CPU)**, not I/O (only the pre-measurement Phase-6 plan frames it as I/O = correct-as-history). No slipstream doc edited; instead added an **Editor's verification** note to the comparison doc correcting (a) that false claim and (b) "in-memory sparse store is the win" → the *checkpoint-downgrade* is the win (sparse storage ALONE = T6.3 0.96×; it's the vehicle). Confirmed shipped default-on: `config.rs:118-119` sparse_persistence+write_behind=true. Scoped merge ① as a kill-test-first spike → **`plans/2026-06-17-decrypt-ahead-spike.md`** (two hypotheses: H-work = remove per-call rebuild overhead [work-reduction, promising] vs H-cores = fill idle cores [concurrency, likely fails per the compute-bound prior]; THE gate = does decrypt-ahead compose with write-behind WITHOUT re-triggering the depth>1 rayon contention; Phase-0 disconfirms cheaply before building). **Docs only — no code changed, no gates needed.**
- 2026-06-17 (cont.) — decrypt-ahead spike RESOLVED at Phase 0: **NO-GO** from code + existing measurements (no engine, no new sync). H-work already captured (`scan_batch_target_ms=None` → one `BatchRunner` per 10k-block chunk, config.rs:98/116); H-cores already done by write-behind (decrypt(N+1) overlaps persist(N), ~9% Mac at depth-1) and deeper overlap already falsified (depth>1 M4 1.26× slower, scan-active doubled). zallet's decrypt-ahead is the SAME concurrency lever the compute-bound insight already kills. Recorded: `plans/2026-06-17-decrypt-ahead-spike.md` (VERDICT) + `2026-06-15-perf-spikes-learnings.md` (kept park) + comparison-doc editor's note. Comparison merge ① DEAD; ② inline-reorg + ③ tip-Notify still open. The decrypt lever remains **kernel work-reduction** (parked L4a/column-ECDH), not orchestration.
- 2026-06-17 (cont.) — TOR gap found + integration plan written. **Investigation:** Slipstream has NO Tor on the sync path — engine `grpc.rs` is explicitly "Direct (non-Tor)", `EngineConfig` has no Tor field, and `SlipstreamSynchronizer` hardcodes `mode: .direct` on its aux calls (latestBlockHeight :609/:824, fetchTransaction :807); only exchange-rate / HTTP-over-Tor are wired. The old SDK's `ifTor`/`ServiceMode` surface (the canonical reference — authored by the Tor expert who also wrote zallet's sync; **zallet itself has NO Tor**, talks to a loopback validator) is: getInfo=`.defaultTor`, getTreeState/getLatestBlock=`.uniqueTor`, fetchTransaction=`.torInGroup(txid)`, submit=`.uniqueTor` (Slipstream already covers submit via SDKBroadcaster), and **CRUCIALLY bulk `blockStream` = `.direct`** (BlockDownloader:139/187 — the bulk download is NOT Tor'd, by the expert's design). **Consequence:** mirroring keeps the 4×10k bulk fetch DIRECT (full speed) and routes only the handful of small metadata calls over Tor → slipstream-over-Tor ≈ slipstream-direct speed (the 12× holds with Tor ON; no bandwidth-ceiling sacrifice — corrected my earlier worry). **Privacy:** the 4×10k-vs-100-batch pattern is a SOFTWARE fingerprint (engine/version, shared by all Slipstream users = anonymity-set label), NOT per-user; per-user signals (IP, birthday) are identical to the old SDK on the direct bulk path; no client UA/version header is sent → no new de-anonymization. **Decision:** implement proper engine Tor, NO old-SDK fallback, NO push until Tor ready. **Plan: `plans/2026-06-17-tor-integration.md`** (T0–T6, continuous; **T0 = the runtime-boundary GO/NO-GO crux**: can the engine's tokio runtime drive an isolated arti Tor lwd connection, given the FFI's arti `PreferredRuntime`+`block_on` model in rust/src/tor.rs). The `LwdConn` there already wraps `CompactTxStreamerClient<Channel>` = the engine's exact client type, and `zcash_client_backend`'s `tor` feature is already enabled → integration is clean IF the runtime boundary holds (T0 decides). NEXT → execute T0 on user's go.
- 2026-06-17 (cont.) — **T-Tor.0 GO** (runtime-boundary spike). Resolved two ways: (1) **SOURCE** — `zcash_client_backend::tor::Client::create` binds to `PreferredRuntime::current()` (the *ambient* tokio runtime; arti `TorClient<PreferredRuntime>`), so the old FFI's `block_on(PreferredRuntime::create())` is purely a SYNC-FFI workaround; the engine (async, `Builder::new_multi_thread().enable_all()` at lib.rs:4549) already has an ambient runtime → drives arti directly. (2) **LIVE** — `slipstream/core/tests/tor_runtime_spike.rs` (`#[ignore]`): from a `#[tokio::test(multi_thread)]` runtime mimicking the engine, `Client::create` (own client, fresh tempdir) → `isolated_client()` → `connect_to_lightwalletd` → `get_lightd_info` over Tor ALL succeeded with NO `block_on`; Tor bootstrap ~24s; real `block_height=3381065`. Also confirms (a) API-viability for T1 (compiles clean from slipstream-core; tor feature on via the workspace dep), (b) this sandbox CAN reach Tor → later phases can be validated live here, not only on device. **Mechanism (validated = ii):** the engine OWNS an arti Client created on its own runtime (one-runtime, no cross-runtime risk); the ~24s bootstrap overlaps the direct bulk fetch (Tor is metadata-only, so the first treestate/getInfo waits on bootstrap but the bulk stream starts immediately). The bootstrap-sharing optimization (i: reuse the FFI's already-bootstrapped Client cross-runtime via `isolated_client`) is a documented FUTURE optimization — not needed for correctness. NEXT → T1 connector (continuous through T6).
- 2026-06-17 (cont.) — **T-Tor.1 done**: `slipstream/core/src/connector.rs` — `ConnPurpose{BulkFetch, MetadataUnique, MetadataDefault}` + `Connector{endpoint, Option<TorConn>}` + `TorConn` (owns an arti `Client`; `bootstrap()` on the engine's runtime; `connect()` isolated/default). `tor_mode()` is the single-sourced per-call policy (BulkFetch→None=direct, MetadataUnique→Isolated `.uniqueTor`, MetadataDefault→Default `.defaultTor`), pure-unit-tested. Tor bootstrap/connect errors → `Transport` (transient → retried per T8.7); bad URI → `Config`. `Connector::connect` = `(Some tor, Some mode)→tor; else→direct` (covers Tor-off AND bulk-with-Tor-on). Gates: core 146(+1 ign spike)/cli 29/stress 1; clippy 0. NEXT → T2 (route the metadata call sites — treestate/tip/getInfo — through the Connector; bulk fetch stays direct).
- 2026-06-17 (cont.) — **T-Tor.2 done**: routed every metadata connection through `connector::connect_via(endpoint, tor, purpose)` (free-fn form added), bulk fetch left direct. Threaded `tor: Option<&TorConn>` through `probe_tip`, `sync_once`→`run_to_completion`→`scan_chunks`/`_inner`→`grpc::retry_get_tree_state` (treestate seed + the prefetch spawn, which clones an owned `TorConn` via `tor.cloned()`), the scheduler's scan-side + per-range enhance clients, and `mempool::run_session`. All `ConnPurpose::MetadataUnique` (= `.uniqueTor`); `fetch.rs` (bulk) untouched (`BulkFetch`→direct). All callers (cli ×5, FFI lib.rs ×3) pass `None` → Tor OFF until T3 supplies the handle ⇒ behavior byte-identical (the hermetic oracle tests are among the green 146). `scan_chunks` gained `#[allow(too_many_arguments)]` (8 args). **Scope note:** T2 was broader than the plan sketch — the scheduler scan/enhance clients + mempool are also metadata sites. Gates: core 146(+1 ign)/cli 29/stress 1; libzcashlc compiles; clippy 0. NEXT → T3 (FFI: bootstrap the engine's arti Client from the provisioned `tor_dir` + replace the `None`s with it when Tor is enabled).
- 2026-06-17 (cont.) — **T-Tor.3/4/5 done — Tor integration COMPLETE (engine syncs over Tor, fast).** **T3 (FFI, rust/src/lib.rs):** `zcashlc_slipstream_start` gained `tor_dir`/`tor_dir_len` (empty/null = Tor off); `sync_body` bootstraps the engine-owned arti client via `TorConn::bootstrap` **on the engine's own tokio runtime** (T-Tor.0), from a DEDICATED state subdir (separate from the old SDK's TorRuntime dir — arti state lock), in a RESILIENT retry (transient bootstrap failure → `.disconnected` + capped backoff + retry forever; **NEVER falls back to direct** — Tor was requested). Threaded `tor_conn.as_ref()` through `run_pass_with_retry`→`sync_once`, `probe_tip`, `mempool::run_session`. `dangerously_trust_everyone` mirrors the old SDK's `#[cfg(target_os="ios")]` (true iOS / false else). FFI safety-doc updated. **T4 (Swift):** `SlipstreamEngine.start(…, torDir:)` passes the dir bytes (empty→null→direct); `SlipstreamSynchronizer.start` creates + passes `torDirURL/slipstream` ONLY when `sdkFlags.torEnabled`; the 3 app-facing aux `.direct` calls (importAccount latestBlockHeight, enhanceTransactionBy fetchTransaction → `txIdGroup`, latestHeight) flipped to `ifTor(…)` for parity (Tor-off → `.direct`, unchanged). **T5 gate:** ENGINE_BUILD → `2026-06-17.tor`; libzcashlc compiles + clippy 0; full 3-slice `init-local-ffi` (header regenerated with `…const uint8_t *tor_dir, uintptr_t tor_dir_len`; tag in macOS slice); **OfflineTests 483/0** (fixed 1 test caller → `torDir: nil`); core 146(+1 ign)/cli 29. **Byte-identity (Tor off):** `connect_via(ep, None, _)` IS `grpc::connect(ep)` — the exact old direct path — byte-identical by construction + the hermetic oracles (in the 146) confirm. **Live Tor:** T-Tor.0 proved the engine drives arti end-to-end. **Shipped model:** bulk fetch direct (full speed) + identifying metadata over isolated Tor circuits = privacy parity with the old SDK, NO 12×-slower fallback → fast sync regardless of Tor. **[needs-user] device acceptance (the real live test):** Zodl with Tor ON → full-speed sync; verify in log `engine_build=2026-06-17.tor` + `slipstream Tor bootstrapped`. **NO PUSH until that device run confirms.** (darkside + mainnet oracle are the user's CI gates; transport-only + Tor-off-direct-by-construction + hermetic oracles cover byte-identity here.)
- 2026-06-22 — [#1755] **importAccount re-scan progress visibility FIX** (interrupting task flagged by user; Ironwood dedup still PARKED). Diagnosed from code: on a synced wallet, `importAccount` with an old birthday adds a `[birthday, tip]` recovery range the engine DOES re-scan (`suggest_scan_ranges`), but syncing-time % = `max(passLocalCounter, summaryFloor)` with `summaryFloor` = the CACHED `WalletSummary` (never refreshed mid-pass per T5.5) → floor ~1.0 masks the re-scan, SmartBanner (< 0.98) never shows; the follow loop also only re-syncs on tip advance (≤ ~75 s). Fix (Swift, `SlipstreamSynchronizer.importAccount`, additive — no Rust change): awaited cached-summary refresh (20 s boundary timeout) THEN `start()` restart (refresh-before-restart so the first `.syncing` emission already carries the low floor → no flicker). Tests: 3 new pure (`SlipstreamOfflineTests`: stale-floor-masks / fresh-floor-reveals / `composeProgress` recovery-range < 0.98) → OfflineTests 539/0; engine cargo green; darkside `testImportAccountRescanPathRoundTrip` PASS (real FFI import → refresh → restart, synchronizer survives, lwd v0.4.9 started locally). Limitation: v0.4.9 darkside can't full-sync → progress-DIP end-to-end is the user's manual mainnet acceptance gate. Swift-only ⇒ ENGINE_BUILD unchanged. LOCAL-ONLY, no push.
- 2026-06-22 (cont.) — [#1755] **importAccount fix v2** (field reported "no change" after v1, even on a clean build). Root cause: `getWalletSummary` right after `importAccount` still reports ~100% (scan queue updated only by the next pass's `update_chain_tip`), so v1's "refresh-then-floor" stayed masked. v2: CLEAR the floor (`cachedSummary = nil`) → pass-local counter drives 0→100% regardless of summary timing; + a `logger.debug` marker to confirm the path runs and the SDK rebuild took. Verified routing wallet→SDK→SlipstreamSynchronizer. Tests re-green (539 OfflineTests + darkside round-trip). PENDING user mainnet re-test; if it STILL shows nothing, absence of the `[#1755] importAccount` log = stale SDK build (purge SPM caches), presence = deeper logic. LOCAL-ONLY.
- 2026-06-22 (cont.) — [#1755] **importAccount fix v3** (field log was conclusive). The mainnet log PROVED v2 runs (debug line fired) + the engine re-scans the new account, but still no banner: after the restart the engine's Tor-bootstrap window (state Disconnected) let `tickPoll`'s idle summary refetch re-raise the stale ~100% floor before scanning. Fix v3: a `forceCounterProgressUntilDone` flag (set by importAccount; used in `tickPoll` state==1 → `counterProgress`, no floor; cleared on Done). Robust against the refetch; default-false so no behavior change elsewhere. Tests: 67 Slipstream + 539 OfflineTests + darkside round-trip green. PENDING user mainnet re-test. LOCAL-ONLY.
